UI block identity — consumer product IS the UI block; it carries, it does not thinkmaster-specThe §1.0 locked schema states: "The operator asks through the UI; the request runs on the Host (Claude CLI — agent runtime + model in one)". The UI sits between Human and Host in the first-layer pipeline Human → UI → Host → Daemon{Orchestration → System of Process} → SoR / External World. The second-order draft fixes the boundary rule on the Host neighbor stub: "the request runs here; the UI carries intent down + results up — it does NOT think (sec.1.0)". Authority + Tenancy "gates every access" — drawn as the dashed wrap around the entire runtime diagram, UI included. The manifest states the page identity criterion verbatim: "consumer-product = UI block identity stated"./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-1-0-runtime

MECE re-cut — Channels · Ask · Receive, Standing surfaces as a Receive sub-case, render-only annotationssecond-order-draftThe UI second order is a MECE re-cut "per founder-accepted DeepSeek review" (WORKING DRAFT 2026-06-11, not locked). Engine parts are three: Channels (transport in/out the operator already uses), Ask (utterance → intent read → journey fan-out), and Receive (how Native talks back). Standing surfaces are folded into Receive as the SUB-CASE "scheduled / proactive Receive": Daily Pulse (H.3), capability map (H.1), proactive capability surfacing (H.2), weekly retro (H.4) — "all land as scheduled / proactive deliveries". Trust moments are folded INSIDE Ask (ask-side) and Receive (receive-side) rather than standing alone. Need-surfaces (5 JTBDs × 4 personas — how surfaces are discovered/justified) and Adaptation (skill modes SM1–SM3 + personalization E.1–E.4) are demoted to RENDER-ONLY annotations that "must NOT leak into the engine as a part". The footer records the full fold: "Standing surfaces folded into Receive, Trust moments folded into Ask/Receive, Need-surfaces + Adaptation demoted to render-only annotations"./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/_consumer-product.html#internal-parts

Ask — 5 intent types, journey fan-out, multi-turn carry, ask-side trust gatingsecond-order-draftEvery utterance resolves to one of 5 intent types (Task / Query / Directive / Feedback / New Idea, B.2) and fans out to journeys C.1–C.5 (B.5). Multi-turn carry holds: recent intent, named entities, active charter, deferred follow-ups, open questions (B.3). Trust moments live INSIDE this flow: confirm-back below confidence threshold (B.4 high/medium/low/very-low ladder) and the ask-before-acting gate (F.1/F.5: irreversible → multi-step confirm; high-risk → one-tap confirm; new authority → grant once/always/no; first-time channel → preview before send). On the Host contract: "Intent passes down only after Ask-side trust gating (confirm-back / ask-before-acting), with Authority + Tenancy gating every access"./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/_consumer-product.html#internal-parts (Ask row)

Receive — 6 modalities, 3 landing times, modality routed by ask shape, receive-side trustsecond-order-draftReceive defines how Native talks back: 6 response modalities (M1 text reply, M2 Slack ping, M3 email, M4 summary card, M5 approval-ask card, M6 scheduled reminder — D.1), 3 landing times (sync / scheduled / async — D.2), modality routing by ask shape (D.4). Standing surfaces are a CASE OF Receive: Daily Pulse (H.3, four fixed blocks Yesterday/Open/Suggested/Signal), capability map (H.1 by-job/by-domain/by-stage), proactive capability surfacing (H.2), weekly retro (H.4). Receive-side trust moments fold in as the M5 approval-ask card, reversibility tags (F.2 reversible / reversible-with-effort / not-reversible), and on-demand audit replay (F.3 "show me what you did and why"). The A+T gate glyph is rendered on the result edge at the M5 approval-card moment — "rendered on edges where approval moments surface, never as a pipeline box"./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/_consumer-product.html#internal-parts (Receive row)

Channels — Day-1 transport in, 3-rule channel order outsecond-order-draftChannels are the "transport in/out the operator already uses". Inbound Day-1 modalities: text chat, Slack mention, email forward, scheduled prompt; voice and screenshot are marked Future (B.1). Outbound channel is picked by the 3-rule order — explicit channel stated > standing preference > default-to-origin — with a one-step escalation override when time-sensitive AND the default channel is async (D.3). Edge labels carry the same contract: "delivery · M1–M6 · 3 landing times" and "channel: explicit > standing > origin (D.3)"./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/_consumer-product.html#internal-parts (Channels row)

Operator-visible states strip — error · empty · onboarding · notificationsecond-order-draftError: misclassified-intent confirm-back with yes/no/clarify (G.1, one focused question max); failed action stated plainly in operator words with 3 recovery options, no stack traces or codes (G.2); operator overrides "stop" / "undo" / "different approach" (G.3); wrong-answer retry loop where two consecutive misses escalate to operator walkthrough (G.4); every correction visibly acknowledged and carried forward, never silent (G.5). Empty is the "I don't know what to ask for" surface: structured capability map by job / domain / stage — live today / available-unused / unlocks-on-grant (H.1); Daily Pulse before the operator opens the surface (H.3); proactive surfacing on adjacent-ask, repeated-manual (3x), and dormant-fit triggers — soft offers that never block and decay after two declines (H.2); re-engagement when dormant via "no new asks in 5 days" (I.6). Onboarding: first session ~10 min ends with one piece of actual work done (I.1), then staged Day 1 → Week 1 → Month 1 (OHS 3–6 band) → Day 90+ (I.2–I.5), with drop-off triggers + recovery moves (I.6: pulse unopened 3 days → shrink to 3 lines). Notification: scheduled deliveries at a stated moment and async completion reports with receipt confirmed up front (D.2); escalation override promotes the channel one step (chat → Slack DM → SMS-tier) and notes why (D.3); cadence/quiet-hours bounds come from the charter (E.2); output discipline defaults to least-noise. All states "land via Receive (M1–M6 deliveries)" per the E7 edge./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/_consumer-product.html#operator-visible-states

Neighbor contracts — Human in/out, Host down/upsecond-order-draftHuman → UI (in): operator utterance — freeform intent resolving to one of 5 intent types, arriving over Day-1 input modalities, with multi-turn context carried across turns. UI → Human (out): delivery in one of 6 modalities at one of 3 landing times, channel selected by 3-rule order with time-sensitive escalation override; carries the operator-visible states (error, empty/capability, onboarding, notification). UI → Host (down): per the locked schema "the operator asks through the UI; the request runs on the Host (Claude CLI — agent runtime + model in one)"; intent passes down only after Ask-side trust gating. Host → UI (up): "Results back up from the platform run — Orchestration translates and finds the process, System of Process runs it gathering context from System of Record + External World and writing output back to the System of Record; the UI delivers those results to the Human via Receive"./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/_consumer-product.html#contracts-with-neighbor-blocks

UI in the pillar × block matrix — B1 PRIMARY homesecond-order-draftIn the pillar × block grid, UI is PRIMARY for B1 "Ask in my own words": "Freeform utterance enters here; parsed intent + schema preview echo back on the same surface within one turn (B1 interaction strip)". B1 Intent canon behind the Ask surface — capture, verify, clarify-never-guessnative-canonCanon B1 defines the NEW primitive Intent { high_level, specifics, expected_output_schema, post_check } — "capture user intent (high-level 'what user wants') and specifics (detailed sub-tasks); pre-LLM declare expected output schema, post-LLM check actual, measure". UX flow: operator utterance arrives via H-Sutra; classifier extracts high_level (verbatim founder voice); on missing specifics a clarification_needed predicate trips and "flows back to operator before continuing". Multi-intent composition (one utterance carries multiple intents) is "not v1; founder may slice manually". The §11.0 worked-sample User viewpoint renders the same contract on the surface: "plain words in — no command, no schema, no form"; Native confirms with a confidence number; "if unclear (conf < 0.6) → ONE clarifying question; never guess"./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/sutra/os/native/blocks/B1-intent-layer.md

Shipped production — boxed decision / approval-ask surface (dir/D20 + dir/D23)shipped-productionProduction D20: "When two or more high-priority items compete for the same resources, the system presents the tension (what competes, why each matters, stakes) as an interactive boxed question and blocks on the operator's choice — it never resolves the conflict autonomously"; enforcement is behavioral-HARD via AskUserQuestion plus CLAUDE.md's 'Highlight decisions visually' presentation contract (ASCII-boxed, impossible to miss). Production D23: on detected conflict with any existing principle, "the system surfaces the conflict naming the specific principle violated and HOLDS — no execution path proceeds until explicit founder approval; absence of approval means no-op, never best-effort". Both dossiers name the existing Native landing machinery: "Native already has the machinery (ADR-009 approval-gate primitive, ESCALATED lifecycle state, M5 approval-ask card)". The D20 redo recipe targets this UI surface directly: "transition to ESCALATED and emit an approval-ask card framed as present-tension-with-options (port the what-competes/why-each-matters/stakes structure verbatim)"; resolution "requires an explicit operator selection event before either item proceeds"./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#dir/D20

Shipped production — Table Shape output contract at the render/presentation stage (cap/115)shipped-productionProduction ships the Table Shape contract as fleet policy (sutra-defaults.json .output_discipline.table_shape, v2.26.0); operative rule verbatim: "Every task list (review/backlog/roadmap/recommendations) carries Impact + Effort columns. Severity/Priority/Score alone insufficient — they rank but do not frame." It defines minimum_header '| # | Area/Task | Impact (who/what changes) | Effort (time, files) |', applies_to task_lists_3_or_more_rows, and applies_to_relayed: subagent_and_codex_output_reshape_before_presenting. Enforcement is convention_only — CSM status 'shipping (policy-visible)' is terminal for this cap. The dossier's redo recipe places it on this block: "Port the rule text and minimum_header string verbatim as a Native output-policy node attached to the render/presentation stage", explicitly including the applies_to_relayed clause as "the differentiating half of the contract and the part with no Native trace"./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#cap/115

_consumer-product gap #4 (stale doc-home refs)RESOLVED 2026-06-12"premise cleared by 4db4c76 anchor cleanup: every doc-home ref points at a named anchor that resolves 1:1 (14 anchors verified). §2.B lives at #pillars, §2.C at #needs. No content edits needed."
Curation surface (§2.F.E.5 "what do you remember about X?" with forget/correct/pin actions) — is it a case of Ask/Receive in the re-cut, or an unassigned operator-visible part? (_consumer-product.html gap 1)
"Empty" state has no literal spec in §2.F — closest grounding is §2.F.H's "I don't know what to ask for"; does a first-open blank-surface behavior need its own spec beyond capability discovery + Daily Pulse? (_consumer-product.html gap 2)
§2.F.G error surfaces never name which Receive modality (M1–M6) carries them — is error rendering a case of M4 summary card / M5 approval-ask card, or an unlisted 7th shape? (_consumer-product.html gap 3)
Notification state spans two homes — outbound landings in §2.F.D but cadence/quiet-hours control in charter §2.F.E.2 — is the state defined as D-modalities + E.2 cadence combined, or does it need a single owning section? (_consumer-product.html gap 5)
B5 says 'operator can write standing prefs directly' — the write path (UI direct to System of Record vs routed through System of Process) is unspecified in the cited ranges. (_matrix.html gap 6)
All 5 pillars' Ring 1 Surface rows (Problem space / Need-outcome / Solution direction) are 'Awaiting founder fill' (T-G1-02..T-G1-16) — could founder voice shift any PRIMARY assignment once filled? UI currently holds B1 PRIMARY. (_matrix.html gap 1)

▸ host — engineering detail

MechanismKindWhat exists (compiled — zero invention)Sources
Host block identity — Claude CLI, agent runtime + model in one (fused)master-specThe §1.0 locked schema: "the request runs on the Host (Claude CLI — agent runtime + model in one)"; the diagram's Host rect carries only the labels "Host" / "Claude CLI" with NO separate LLM box. The second-order draft draws agent runtime and model as one fused unit — "single block, no internal edge to a separate LLM box — the doc explicitly collapses them". The Host then carries the request downward into the Daemon on the edge labeled "runs", where Orchestration translates it and selects the process. A return edge comes back up from System of Process: when a process runs, it invokes a host LLM selected per workflow — "so the Host slot is entered from below as well as above". Everything sits inside the Authority + Tenancy gate wrap and stands on the Compute base layer./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-1-0-runtime
/Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/_block-host.html#internal-flow
Internal parts — agent runtime · model · per-workflow host-LLM slotsecond-order-draftAgent runtime: "the agent-execution half of Claude CLI"; "the doc gives no further sub-decomposition (no tool loop, session, or context-window detail in the cited ranges)". Model: "the LLM, fused into the same block as the runtime — 'agent runtime + model in one'". Per-workflow host-LLM slot: "the Host position is a selectable slot, not hardwired to Claude" — ADR-005 host-llm-host-selection (ratified), with the draft quoting the master §12.1 ADR-table label "Per-workflow host LLM selection (claude · codex · deepseek)" and §4.0's canon-link citing ADR-005 as part of the shipped daemon runtime at sutra/marketplace/native/./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/_block-host.html#internal-parts
/Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/master/index.html#so-host
Neighbor contracts — UI in, Daemon out (runs + lifecycle), SoP host-LLM return, A+T wrap, Compute basesecond-order-draftUI → Host (in): operator request — "The operator asks through the UI; the request runs on the Host" (unlabeled §1.0 arrow). Host → Daemon (out): request handoff into the always-on Daemon where Orchestration "translates the request and finds the relevant process to run"; the draft NOTES the §1.0 edge is labeled "runs" (Host runs the Daemon), not "forwards" — "the doc fuses request-flow and run-relationship on one edge". Host → Daemon (lifecycle): "Started by Sutra plugin at session boot OR by explicit sutra native start. Re-exec across plugin updates without state loss"; lifecycle START → RUNNING → STOP → RESTART (state restored from user-kit + decision-provenance log). System of Process → Host (in): host-LLM invocation per workflow (ADR-005) — "the running process calls back into a host LLM chosen per workflow". Authority + Tenancy gates every access (cross-cutting wrap, Host included); Compute carries the runs-on relationship — "base layer, no data payload"./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/_block-host.html#contracts-neighbor-edges
/Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-0-daemon
ADR-005 — per-workflow host-LLM selection (claude --bare vs codex exec)adr-decisionDecision: "Native engine MUST require WorkflowStep.host ∈ {claude, codex} whenever action='invoke_host_llm'". host='claude' invokes claude --bare — recursion-safe (no plugin sync, no CLAUDE.md auto-load, no hooks; skips keychain reads so unauthenticated for connector calls); default for steps that must execute in the founder's session via the effector split (ADR-004). host='codex' invokes codex exec — read-only sandbox; default for analysis, review, planning steps producing JSON/text without state change. Per-step timeout_ms flows into host activity args; host-class default 60s for claude --bare, ≥300s for codex (per Wave 1 evidence on DayFlow). Context for the lock: recursive Claude without --bare "re-loads the same plugin governance pipeline that produced the Workflow, doubling token cost AND potentially recursing forever". Tradeoff locked to these two hosts in v1.x; the LLMSubstrate adapter for OpenAI/Gemini swap is deferred to backlog OS-21; OS-20 (per-invocation MCP context passing for claude --bare) also deferred. Consequence rows: "Workflow author chooses the trust profile per step explicitly (no hidden default)"; "Steps that need BOTH authenticated mutation AND sandboxed analysis must split into two steps"./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/sutra/os/decisions/ADR-005-host-llm-host-selection.md#decision
/Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/sutra/os/decisions/ADR-005-host-llm-host-selection.md#consequences
ADR-004 — registry-and-effector split: the Host session is the effectoradr-decisionDecision: "Native engine MUST be the registry + audit layer; the founder's interactive host-LLM session MUST be the effector that performs declared mutations". Native owns Workflow JSON, Trigger predicates, Execution rows, EngineEvent audit, DecisionProvenance, Tenant isolation; Claude (founder session) owns filesystem writes, git operations, network calls — "driven by Workflow steps but executed in the authenticated session". The split is recorded on every Execution via the agent_identity chain. Consequences: daemon stays unauthenticated and recursion-safe (no credential exfil surface); founder retains ASK-gate visibility on every mutation; audit records intent BEFORE the Claude effector runs (replayable); autonomous fire-and-forget workflows are blocked at v1.0 (deferred to v1.3 per OS-1/OS-2). RUN canon states the same boundary: "The host-LLM session is the effector boundary (§5.1) — file/git/network mutations declared in the Workflow happen there, not in Native. Native persists EngineEvents; the host writes the actual side effects."/Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/sutra/os/decisions/ADR-004-registry-and-effector-split.md#decision
/Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/sutra/os/native/surfaces/run.md#c4-context
ADR-021 — Sutra plugin is HOST-RESIDENT (Host gap #4 CLOSED)adr-decisionDecision (Accepted 2026-06-12, founder-delegated, parity T5): "The Sutra plugin is HOST-RESIDENT. It is the governance layer the Host (Claude CLI) loads per-session" — hooks = Host lifecycle interceptors (PreToolUse/PostToolUse/Stop/SessionStart/UserPromptSubmit); skills = Host-invokable bundles (slash-command surface); per-turn blocks = Host-emitted discipline (header, routing, depth, blueprint, build-layer, trace); sutra-defaults.json = policy payload the Host-resident layer carries. Daemon-side analogs (workflow registry, EngineEvents, schedules) live in Orchestration / System of Process; upstream governance CONTENT the plugin transports is Authority+Tenancy material — "the plugin is its transport, not its home"; no new block is added and §1.0 stays locked. Consequences: parity ledger rows for plugin-implemented concepts anchor to Host (+ projection to their content's altitude); future plugin capabilities declare Host-residency by default; "the 17 shipped-but-unwired hooks (parity T1 finding) are Host-resident dormant mechanisms; wire-or-retire review is an ops follow-up, not a placement question". Master §4.12.4 records the same: status ACCEPTED 2026-06-12, Host block open gap #4 CLOSED; dual-lane — deepseek reviewed this run, codex retro queued (auth)./Users/asawa/Claude/asawa-holding/sutra/os/decisions/ADR-021-sutra-plugin-host-residency.md#decision

Daemon lifecycle via the Host-resident plugin (§4.0)master-specNative runs as a per-operator local daemon the operator doesn't see; home directory ~/.sutra-native/ contains user-kit (charters, domains, triggers, workflows), decision-provenance.jsonl, native.log. Process model: "Single background daemon process per operator. Started by Sutra plugin at session boot OR by explicit sutra native start. Re-exec across plugin updates without state loss" — operator-visible: "No (managed by plugin)". Lifecycle: START (plugin launch / cron / cold boot) → RUNNING (workflow dispatches + hook firings + cadence ticks + audit writes) → STOP (plugin shutdown / explicit stop) → RESTART (state restored from user-kit + decision-provenance log). Only-SoP-calls-Host exclusivity is a working assumption carried from a founder session (echoed in _matrix.html / _block-orchestration.html, not source of truth) — "this exclusivity appears nowhere in index.html — §1.0 states no such rule, and ADR-005 only says host-LLM selection is per-workflow. Confirm and write it down (§1.0 or an ADR), or drop it." (_block-host.html gap 1)
ADR-005 allows codex/deepseek as host-LLMs (per the master §12.1 label), yet §1.0 labels the Host box "Claude CLI" singular — is Claude CLI the fixed operator-facing host while per-workflow LLM calls may route to a different host, or can the top-of-stack Host itself be swapped? Cited ranges do not reconcile this. (_block-host.html gap 2; note ADR-005's own enum is {claude, codex})
§1.0's Host → Daemon edge label "runs" conflates lifecycle (Host runs the Daemon) with request flow (request reaches Orchestration) — which transport carries the request (CLI invocation, IPC, file)? Not specified in cited ranges. (_block-host.html gap 3)
Host is per-session while Daemon is "always-on" — what happens to an in-flight operator request if the Host (Claude CLI session) exits while the Daemon is still RUNNING? Cited ranges cover daemon restart but not Host-absence semantics. (_block-host.html gap 5; manifest acceptance: "Host-absence semantics gap preserved")
No internal decomposition of the Host beyond "agent runtime + model in one" exists anywhere in the cited ranges — is second-order detail for the Host block (session state, tool loop, context window) intentionally deferred to the UI/_consumer-product detailing pass? (_block-host.html gap 6)
Which block issues B1's intent-parse LLM call? §4.1 puts LLM calls inside Workflow Steps (SoP runtime) and ADR-005 selects the host-LLM per workflow, yet B1's parse contract sits in Orchestration's translate step. (_matrix.html gap 2)

Block contract — translate the request, find the process (§1.0 locked schema)master-spec§1.0 states the runtime at a glance: "the operator asks through the UI; the request runs on the Host (Claude CLI — agent runtime + model in one); inside the always-on Daemon, Orchestration translates the request and finds the relevant process to run; the System of Process runs it (this is the runtime)". The §1.0 diagram edge Host→Daemon is labeled "runs"; the Orchestration→System-of-Process edge is labeled "task = process". Boundary contracts — Host in, Host confirm-back out, SoP out, Authority gatessecond-order-draftHost (in): operator request dispatched into the Daemon, arriving as freeform utterance via Day-1 modalities — text chat, Slack mention, email forward, scheduled prompt (voice + screenshot future, per §2.F.B.1). Host (out): confirm-back / ASK BACK to the operator when confidence is below threshold (§2.F.B.4–B.5) — clarification question, operator answers, flow resumes; the B1 interaction strip also shows a schema preview returned before execution ({ metrics:[], sources:[], format:one-pager }). System of Process (out): the selected process on the "task = process" edge — carried content per B1 acceptance is parsed intent_type + structured sub-intents (typed intent) against a pre-declared output schema; System of Process then runs it as THE runtime. Authority + Tenancy (gates): "Authority + Tenancy gates every access". The draft notes §1.0 shows no upward return edge Orchestration→Host for the confirm-back (gap 6).holding/website/native/_block-orchestration.html (Contracts table)

Internal parts — 8-part intent machinery (second-order, fully wired)second-order-draftThe second-order draft wires eight internal parts. Trigger intake: utterance · cadence (cron) · event per §4.1 Trigger primitive; the daemon fires scheduled triggers (§4.0), so clocked input enters the same translate/find-the-process path. Request translator: first half of the stated job ("Orchestration translates the request"), the group frame holding the intent machinery. Intent Layer: per B1 Ring 2 contract — parses freeform utterance into typed intent + sub-intents, pre-declared output schema shown before the LLM call, post-LLM output validated and rejected if non-conforming, plus show-by-example and trusted-names shortcut. Intent classifier: every utterance resolves to one of five types — Task, Query, Directive, Feedback, New Idea — each distinguished by a 'tell' (§2.F.B.2). Multi-turn context carry: five things persist across turns/sessions — recent intents (last 3-5 asks), named entities, active charter, deferred follow-ups, open questions (§2.F.B.3). Confidence gate: four bands — high → proceed; medium → yes/no confirm; low → "I read this as a task — is that right?"; very low → "act, answer, or remember?" (§2.F.B.4). Intent→journey fan-out: TASK→§C.1 · QUERY→§C.2 · DIRECTIVE→§C.3 · FEEDBACK→§C.4 · NEW IDEA→§C.5 · UNKNOWN→ASK BACK (§2.F.B.5). Process finder: second half of the stated job — "finds the relevant process to run"; matching mechanism not named in the cited master ranges (draft gap 2).holding/website/native/_block-orchestration.html (Internal parts table)
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-2-f-2-input-intent
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-1-primitives
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-0-daemon
B1 Intent primitive — typed intent with pre/post gate (canon)native-canonCanon B1 defines the NEW primitive Intent { high_level, specifics, expected_output_schema, post_check } — "the testing-framework analog at the intent layer". Flow: classifier extracts high_level verbatim; decomposer (B2) extracts specifics; the Intent primitive is instantiated with both plus a Workflow-author-declared expected_output_schema; on Workflow completion post_check evaluates output against the intent schema — pass → workflow_completed, fail → routes via canon on_failure per §6.5. Acceptance: schema is captured at Workflow registration (W-hash content-addressed); specifics missing trips a clarification_needed predicate that flows back to the operator before continuing. Edge cases: no expected_output_schema → Workflow rejected at registration; multi-intent utterance is not v1 (canon-silent gap per F2). B1 pairs with B7: B7 is the per-LLM-step gate, B1 the per-INTENT gate framing the whole Workflow.sutra/os/native/blocks/B1-intent-layer.md#scope-in--out
sutra/os/native/blocks/B1-intent-layer.md#acceptance-criteria-givenwhenthen
sutra/os/native/blocks/B1-intent-layer.md#data-model
ROUTE surface — classify utterance → matched Workflow OR pattern proposalnative-canonROUTE is the body of NativeEngine.handleHSutraEvent: the H-Sutra event bus (§5.3) tails the per-turn JSONL log and HSutraConnector forwards each row; Router.route(evt) returns a RoutingDecision synchronously (routeAsync is the latent path per §8 OS-8). Invariants: ROUTE-I1 — every HSutraEvent produces exactly one routing_decision EngineEvent (matched or unmatched); ROUTE-I2 — routing_decision.execution_id is null (routing happens outside any Execution); ROUTE-I3 — a matched target_workflow_id MUST resolve in the registry; ROUTE-I4 — no match makes the utterance a candidate for the pattern detector (k counter increment), no Workflow fires; ROUTE-I5 (2026-06-12 PROTO-006 lesson-port amendment) — routing precedes every operator-originated action, with the operative ladder verbatim: "Process exists? → follow it. Cannot follow? → resolve without skipping. Still blocked? → STOP, ask human. Never write 'TBD.'" Falsification test: any trace showing a Workflow fired without a preceding routing_decision, or a blocked step resolved by silent skip, proves ROUTE-I5 violated.sutra/os/native/surfaces/route.md#purpose
sutra/os/native/surfaces/route.md#invariants-must-always-hold

routing_decision event (#1 of 26) — schema, emitter, consumers, fail-closednative-canonEmitted unconditionally for every HSutraEvent that reaches the Router — including no-match outcomes — so every founder turn has a routing audit trail. CloudEvents 1.0 form with payload { execution_id: null, matched_workflow_id (W-hash or null), predicate_id, score, agent_identity, ts_ms }. Router is the exclusive emitter. Consumers: AUDIT always persists per ADR-013 3-channel durability; LiteExecutor consumes a matched decision and prepares the Execution (causal predecessor of precondition_check #10 and workflow_started #2); the EMERGE pattern detector counts no-match events for k≥4 emergence detection. When matched_workflow_id is null the event is terminal for that HSutraEvent. Fail-closed on emission failure: if the append fails across all 3 durability channels, HS-4 fires — governance hooks BLOCK; no fail-open semantic.sutra/os/native/events/routing_decision.md#purpose
sutra/os/native/events/routing_decision.md#schema-cloudevents-10-form
sutra/os/native/events/routing_decision.md#replayability
Process finding via TriggerSpec registry (canon mechanism behind the 'process finder')native-canonCanon TriggerSpec declares WHEN a Workflow fires: pattern ∈ {preprocessor, observer, gate, fan_out, negotiation}, a typed predicate (match-all / match-any / cron — NEVER free prose, ADR-012 PNC parser, HARD mint-time reject), a target_workflow_id that MUST resolve in the user-kit registry (runtime HARD reject — no firing — if the target is later deprecated), and an optional cadence object per ADR-017. "The Router uses TriggerSpecs to map operator utterances (and cadence ticks) to target Workflows." Active utterance-driven path: Router consults Triggers for every HSutraEvent; on match it emits routing_decision and dispatches NativeEngine.run(target_workflow_id, ctx). Match miss: Router emits routing_decision with no matched_workflow_id, or routes to the pattern-proposal flow per ADR-010 if k≥4 unmatched utterances accumulate. An INDEX.jsonl at ~/.sutra-native/user-kit/triggers/ enumerates {id, pattern, target_workflow_id, has_cadence, ts_minted_ms} for Router fast-path lookup.sutra/os/native/primitives/trigger.md#purpose

ADR-010 — no-match path: organic emergence propose-approve at k≥4adr-decisionDecision: "Native engine MUST require explicit founder approval via approve P-<id> utterance to register any pattern-detected Workflow; the detector MUST propose only at k≥4 utterance matches." The pattern detector reads the H-Sutra JSONL (ADR-013) and tracks per-pattern hit count + sample utterances; at k≥4 with no registered workflow matching, it emits pattern_proposed (event 12) with draft Workflow JSON + samples. Founder approve P-<id> → proposal_approved (event 13) → register Workflow; reject P-<id> <reason> → proposal_rejected (event 14). Threshold rationale: k=2 too noisy, k=3 still ambiguous, k=4 is the smallest k where a pattern is consistently a real workflow; auto-register was rejected (no oversight — can mint workflows from typos), k≥5 rejected (founder loses the "Native noticed!" moment). D45 established organic emergence as a v1.2 goal that must remain founder-gated.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-010-organic-emergence-propose-approve.md#context

§11.0 B1 worked sample — the 4-viewpoint engineering shape for intent intakemaster-specMaster §11.0 ships the L3 drill-down for S1 Intent (B1) as the WORKED SAMPLE every other subsystem reuses. User viewpoint: "plain words in — no command, no schema, no form"; Native confirms ("Got it — TASK, routing to Action. Confidence 0.81") and "if unclear … ONE clarifying question; never guess". System viewpoint: raw_text → [Intake Capture] → .tmp/native-utterance.txt → [Classifier] (single LLM call) → { intent, confidence, routed_to, clarify } → intake_log.jsonl (fsync, append-only, ADR-013); conf >= 0.6 gates a clarify loop vs EngineEvent intake.classified routed to B2/B3. AI viewpoint: prompt = system(role + HOW dims) + utterance + trusted-names; intent ∈ {ask, task, direction, feedback} + sub-intents + confidence; HOW dimension selected ("decompose" vs "direct" vs "consult"); EVAL GATE: golden set (50) >= 90% correct-route as the agentic accept bar; low-conf → clarify ("test-before-conclude: never assert a guess"). Everything viewpoint stitches: runs inside PLATFORM (daemon), routes to B2/B3 by intent_type, gated by B5 GOVERN (authority + HS), every turn logged to OBSERVABILITY.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-11-0-subsystems
D17 — capture ALL founder statements; multi-intent decomposition is the missing B1 mechanismshipped-productionDoctrine: "Capture ALL founder statements in rapid succession… Check message queue before responding"; enforcement row: agent must enumerate all statements before responding — count(actions) must cover count(statements). The hard substrate that ships is capture-prompt.sh (plugin L0, UserPromptSubmit): losslessly appends every founder prompt as {ts, session_id, prompt} to holding/state/prompts/YYYY-MM.jsonl with kill-switches and never-block semantics, so a missed statement is permanently recoverable. Redo recipe names the Orchestration-side mechanism production never built: a multi-intent decomposition step in the B1 intent layer that splits one turn into N statements, emits one queued work item per statement, and runs a coverage check (work items >= statements) before the turn closes. Gap the source states: B1 intent canon explicitly defers multi-intent composition ('not v1; founder may slice manually'); no capture-all-parallel-statements contract or statements-vs-actions coverage check exists in the Native runtime.holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#dird17

cap/116 PROTO-006 — process-discipline routing ladder (shipped, now ported as ROUTE-I5)shipped-productionProduction ships PROTO-006 as constitutional protocol + fleet schema: trigger = agent receives a task OR cannot complete a required process step; check = "Process exists? -> follow it. Cannot follow? -> resolve without skipping. Still blocked? -> STOP, ask human. Never write 'TBD.'"; fleet schema rule = "When in doubt about whether to ship: route via dispatcher — read SUTRA-CONFIG/CLAUDE.md, classify input (TYPE/ROUTE), emit Depth + BLUEPRINT, then act. No silent skips." Only the named founder override phrases ('skip the process' / 'just do it' / 'skip depth assessment') suspend it. The dossier's redo recipe — wire it into the workflow engine's pre-action decision points rather than leaving it memory-only, and resolve the HARD-vs-SOFT label inconsistency — landed in canon on 2026-06-12 as the ROUTE-I5 amendment: every HSutraEvent passes through ROUTE before RUN or EMERGE, and no bypass handler exists. ▸ system-of-process — engineering detail

MechanismKindWhat exists (compiled — zero invention)Sources
Block contract — runs the process; THE runtime (§1.0 + §4 header)master-spec§1.0: "the System of Process runs it (this is the runtime), gathering context from the System of Record + the External World and writing output back to the System of Record"; "Second-level detail (context-build · verify · connectors) lives inside System of Process — see §4". §4 header defines purpose: "The substrate that scopes, deploys, and supervises a workforce of agents. CoS (and future products) ride on it." Owns: Charters (fixed/flex) · Agents (on-demand materialization) · Method routing per task tier · Authority gates · State envelope between pillars · Audit substrate (DecisionProvenance). Depends on §5 Connector layer (any out-of-process tool call) and §7 Observability (instruments platform health). Does NOT own: operator surface, pillar UX, pricing, channels. "Native is the engine room. Operators don't see Native directly — they see CoS."holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-1-0-runtime

Boundary contracts — Orchestration, SoR, External World, Host, Authority, Observabilitysecond-order-draftFrom the second-order draft contracts table: Orchestration (in) — "task = process"; at runtime granularity this arrives as a Workflow dispatch on Trigger fire (operator utterance, cadence cron, or event). System of Record (bidirectional) — "context + output": reads operator context (context_envelope my_own; user-kit markdown Charter/Domain/Workflow specs as authoritative state per ADR-008); writes back artifacts + ExecutionResults + b5_writes + triple-channel audit (DecisionProvenance JSONL gate-log/provenance/artifact). External World (bidirectional) — "context + effects" via the Connector layer; outbound external-effect calls (send / write / delete / pay) each pass the Authority gate; inbound responses return into envelope outbound[].inbound_responses; connector half-failures drop the workflow into DEGRADED. Host (out) — LLM calls at Step granularity; the Step primitive (§4.1) is the doc's only LLM-call site, which implies System of Process is the Host caller; an explicit only-SoP-calls-Host rule is not yet stated anywhere in the doc (open gap). Authority + Tenancy (gates) — Authority gate fires at every external-effect call; escalations captured as Approval primitive; cross-tenant default-deny with explicit delegates_to edge required (HS-3 + ADR-006). Observability (out) — accuracy KPI (% of outputs passing first-time test vs needing retry); daemon health surfaced only via Daily Pulse health row; audit replay surfaces.holding/website/native/_block-system-of-process.html (Contracts table)
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-1-primitives
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-4-acceptance
Runtime daemon substrate (§4.0)master-specNative runs as a per-operator local background daemon the operator doesn't see; it manages workforce deployment, audit-log writes, hook firing, and cadence scheduling silently. Home directory ~/.sutra-native/ — user-kit (charters, domains, triggers, workflows), decision-provenance.jsonl, native.log. Process model: single background daemon per operator, started by the Sutra plugin at session boot or by explicit sutra native start; re-exec across plugin updates without state loss. Lifecycle: START → RUNNING (handles workflow dispatches + hook firings + cadence ticks + audit writes) → STOP → RESTART (state restored from user-kit + decision-provenance log); operator-visible only via the Daily Pulse health row. State persistence: 3-channel audit (DecisionProvenance JSONL + stderr beacon + tmp fallback per HS-4 + ADR-013); user-kit markdown files are authoritative state per ADR-008. Boundary: per-Tenant isolated (HS-3 + ADR-006); cross-tenant calls require explicit delegates_to edge; default-deny. Shipped runtime lives under sutra/marketplace/native/ (CLI launcher bin/sutra-native + src/), path corrected 2026-05-13 per §10.10 P1 #5.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-0-daemon
10 typed primitives — and what is NOT a primitive (§4.1)master-specNative runtime uses 10 typed primitives per canon at sutra/os/native/primitives/: Domain, Charter, Workflow, Step, Trigger, ExecutionResult, EngineEvent (26-event catalog), Tenant, Approval, DecisionProvenance. Step is "where work happens": pre-declared expected output + LLM call OR deterministic action + post-call check (per P2 pre/post-LLM-validation). Trigger is what fires a Workflow — operator utterance, cadence (cron), or event; trigger spec lives in the Workflow file. ExecutionResult is what a Workflow produced — typed output artifact, status, audit row references; operator-readable summary, system-readable for next iteration (P1 artifact-first). Explicitly NOT primitives anymore: "Agent" (composed from Workflow + Step + Charter scope + Authority gate), "Method routing" (per-Step config + HOW Engine in §3), "Audit substrate" (replaced by DecisionProvenance + 3-channel ADR-013).holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-1-primitives
holding/website/native/_block-system-of-process.html (internal parts table, '10 typed primitives' row)
Workflow primitive — content-addressed, typed-gated unit of operator work (canon)native-canonWorkflow encodes the WHAT (steps), the WHEN (triggers via TriggerSpec), the GATING (preconditions / postconditions / requires_approval / failure_policy), and the OWNERSHIP (custody_owner / tenant boundaries). id = sha256(canonical_form(workflow)) — content-addressed W-hash; any field change yields a new Workflow id (immutable; new mint required). Invariants: I-5 step_graph reachable from step[0] via failure_policy edges (T1-T6 terminal checks — no orphan steps, no unreachable terminal, no infinite loops without explicit continue); F-13 reuse_tag=true requires non-null return_contract (mint-time rejection); F-7 modifies_sutra=true requires reflexive_check Constraint cleared or HS-1 fires; ADR-012 — preconditions/postconditions MUST parse as typed PNC predicates, free prose rejected at mint-time. Lifecycle: Mint → Dormant → Triggered → Running → Terminal (per I-14 exactly one of {workflow_completed, workflow_failed, approval_requested}); rollback events are RECOVERY transitions after workflow_failed, NOT terminal; Decommission via deprecated=true prevents new Executions. Registry rows at ~/.sutra-native/user-kit/workflows/<id>.json with INDEX.jsonl.sutra/os/native/primitives/workflow.md#purpose
sutra/os/native/primitives/workflow.md#invariants-must-hold

WorkflowStep primitive — smallest dispatchable unit; the only LLM-call site (canon)native-canonEach Step is either a skill_ref (named pre-registered Skill) XOR an action (registered action kinds: invoke_host_llm, emit_event, etc.) — never both (I-3; F-4 + F-5 forbid both-null and both-non-null; mint-time HARD reject). host ∈ {claude, codex} and prompt_template are required IFF action === 'invoke_host_llm' (ADR-005). on_failure defaults to the parent Workflow.failure_policy (same enum-of-5 per ADR-011); every inputs[i]/outputs[i] carries schema_ref + authoritative_status per ADR-008. Approval gate: requires_approval=true pauses BEFORE dispatching the action — emits approval_requested (#15), persists a ledger row at user-kit/pending-approvals/E-<id>.json (I-15), Execution state → awaiting_approval; resume via approve E-<id> utterance → NativeEngine.resumeApproved(execId). Dispatch kinds: invoke_host_llm spawns a host subprocess; emit_event writes an EngineEvent row; skill_ref resolves to a sub-Workflow via SkillEngine.resolve. Step terminal-events (step_completed, step_paused) are NOT in I-14's terminal set — I-14 binds the parent Execution, not individual Steps.sutra/os/native/primitives/step.md#purpose
sutra/os/native/primitives/step.md#invariants-must-hold

ExecutionResult — the runtime instantiation IS the audit trail (canon)native-canonExecutionResult is a content-addressed E-<hash> row capturing the trigger that fired it, the running/terminal state, the append-only EngineEvent log, per-step results, parent/sibling lineage (parent_exec_id, sibling_group for fan_out groups), failure reason, agent_identity chain (ADR-015), tenant_context (ADR-006), and a partial flag for failure_policy='continue' semantics; "every founder-meaningful change to the system flows through some ExecutionResult; ExecutionResult IS the audit trail". State enum is the 6-set {running, success, failed, awaiting_approval, paused, declared_gap}. Invariants: I-4 — failure_reason === null IFF state ∈ {success, declared_gap} (HARD-checked at terminal_check); I-14 — exactly one terminal EngineEvent from {workflow_completed, workflow_failed, approval_requested}; I-15 — awaiting_approval requires a persisted pending-approvals ledger row; I-7 — ≥1 DecisionProvenance per consequential decision; I-8 — tenant_context.tenant_id non-null when crossing tenants, cross-tenant without delegates_to is an HS-3 violation; logs[] is append-only, replay-safe. Failure routing per Step.on_failure: abort → workflow_failed; escalate → workflow_escalated (#24); rollback → workflow_failed then recovery events #19-#23; pause → state paused (non-terminal); continue → partial=true and advance.sutra/os/native/primitives/execution-result.md#purpose

RUN surface — fire matched Workflow, dispatch steps, host-LLM at the effector boundary (canon)native-canonRUN: "Fire a matched Workflow → dispatch each WorkflowStep → for action='invoke_host_llm' steps, hand the prompt to the configured host (claude --bare or codex exec) → persist a typed Execution log." Two operator paths: via ROUTE matched-workflow (NativeEngine.run(workflowId, ctx) from handleHSutraEvent, §3.1) and via CLI (sutra-native run <workflow-id>, §3.3). Invariants RUN-I1..I7: exactly one workflow_started per fired Workflow; every step dispatch emits exactly one step_started and one of step_completed/step_paused/step-failed routing; terminal Execution emits exactly one of workflow_completed/workflow_failed/rollback_complete/rollback_partial; invoke_host_llm requires host ∈ {claude, codex} and non-null prompt_template; failure_policy ∈ 5-set; per-step timeout_ms defaults to host-class default — 60s for claude --bare, ≥300s expected for complex codex tasks per §6.7. "RUN executes within the Native daemon. The host-LLM session is the effector boundary (§5.1) — file/git/network mutations declared in the Workflow happen there, not in Native. Native persists EngineEvents; the host writes the actual side effects." Canon gap: NativeEngine.on_host_llm_result(execId, stepIdx, r) is the resumption callback but the exact wire is open per §8 OS-1.sutra/os/native/surfaces/run.md#purpose
sutra/os/native/surfaces/run.md#invariants-must-always-hold
sutra/os/native/surfaces/run.md#c4-context
ADR-004 + ADR-005 — registry-and-effector split; host selection two-knob lockadr-decisionADR-004 decision: "Native engine MUST be the registry + audit layer; the founder's interactive host-LLM session MUST be the effector that performs declared mutations." Native owns Workflow JSON, Trigger predicates, Execution rows, EngineEvent audit, DecisionProvenance, Tenant isolation; Claude (founder session) owns filesystem writes, git operations, network calls; the split is recorded on every Execution via the agent_identity chain (ADR-015); v1.3 may add per-Workflow sandbox config but the split remains the default. Rationale: host: claude --bare skips keychain reads (recursion-safe but unauthenticated) and host: codex defaults to a read-only sandbox. ADR-005 decision: "Native engine MUST require WorkflowStep.host ∈ {claude, codex} whenever action='invoke_host_llm'" — claude --bare is recursion-safe (no plugin sync, no CLAUDE.md auto-load, no hooks), default for effector-split execution; codex exec is read-only sandbox, default for analysis/review/planning steps; the two hosts have orthogonal trust profiles (bare = no recursion, codex = no mutation). The lock is v1.x; LLMSubstrate adapter for OpenAI/Gemini swap is deferred to backlog OS-21.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-004-registry-and-effector-split.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-005-host-llm-host-selection.md#decision
ADR-011 + ADR-012 — closed 5-set failure policy; typed PNC predicates over proseadr-decisionADR-011: "Native engine MUST define step.on_failure as exactly one of {rollback, escalate, pause, abort, continue} — closed 5-set, no extension without ADR." rollback compensates steps in reverse (workflow_rollback_started → _complete or _partial); escalate emits workflow_escalated and routes to the founder channel; pause emits step_paused, persists a queue entry, resumes on signal; abort emits workflow_failed immediately; continue logs the failure, advances to step[i+1], sets partial=true. Workflow.failure_policy is the default; step.on_failure overrides per-step; each policy has a typed event family (events 4, 7, 19-24) — no free-form failure log; a sixth policy requires a new ADR + new event types + parser + executor branch (extension cost is deliberate). ADR-012: "Native engine MUST parse Workflow.preconditions, Workflow.postconditions, Workflow.failure_policy, and step.on_failure as typed machine-checkable predicates — not free prose"; parser surface is match-all / match-any / cron / typed comparison against execution context; evaluated at runtime via precondition_check (#10) and postcondition_check (#11); commitment_broken (#25) references a Charter obligation id resolvable in the registry (I-16); F-10 forbids English-only routing/gating positions (HARD reject at primitive-mint). Evidence: the 13-case run report showed prose PNC declared at mint but never eval-checked at runtime — silent guarantee failure.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-011-on-failure-policy-five-set.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-012-pnc-typed-parser-over-prose.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-012-pnc-typed-parser-over-prose.md#context
ADR-009 — approval gate as Workflow-level primitive (typed pause, ledger, utterance resume)adr-decisionDecision: "Native engine MUST add requires_approval: bool at both Workflow and WorkflowStep levels, and emit/handle the typed event triplet approval_requested → approval_granted | approval_denied." A step with requires_approval=true reaching the executor emits approval_requested; the Execution transitions to awaiting_approval; a ledger row persists at user-kit/pending-approvals/E-<id>.json (I-15). Founder utterance approve E-<id> → NativeEngine.resumeApproved(execId) → continue at step_index+1; reject E-<id> <reason> → approval_denied → terminal workflow_failed. Idempotent: re-fire on a resolved approval emits approval_already_handled (I-14 holds — exactly one terminal event per Execution). Context: an inline Y/N stdin gate is physically impossible (the daemon is detached from stdin, no controlling TTY); re-execute-from-scratch on approval was rejected as wasteful + non-replayable. Multi-party approval / quorum predicate is deferred (OS-15).sutra/os/decisions/ADR-009-approval-gate-primitive.md#decision

ADR-017 — cadence scheduling: daemon-side setInterval tick, never external cronadr-decisionDecision: "Native engine MUST implement cadence scheduling as a daemon-side setInterval tick; MUST NOT depend on external cron / launchd." CadenceScheduler.start(intervalMs) runs inside the daemon process; on each tick, scheduled cadences whose next-fire time falls within the ±5 min window (I-12) fire TriggerEvent rows routed to the Workflow registry; each fire emits an EngineEvent + DecisionProvenance — replayable like any other Trigger. External cron was rejected for three sourced problems: macOS launchd vs Linux cron has no portable CLI surface; external cron fires are not Executions and carry no DecisionProvenance; the ±5 min tolerance (I-12) is hard to enforce across OS schedulers. A cron-string escape hatch is partially accepted as the TriggerSpec.cadence.cron field, evaluated by the daemon at tick time. Cadence examples in the charter table: per-hour Sutra-OS monitoring, per-6h team tick, daily Asawa pulse, weekly drift report, monthly comprehensive audit, quarterly stress-test.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-017-cron-daemon-cadence-tick.md#decision

State machine, state envelope, and how a run flows (§4.2 + §4.3 + draft narrative)master-spec§4.2 happy path: PROPOSED (operator adds Charter) → ACTIVE (Charter live; new task T0–T3 arrives) → agent materializes scoped to that Charter → task completes → PERSISTED (artifact + audit row; output → B5.a-d) → RETIRED (operator revokes / scope expires / superseded; audit replayable forever). §4.3: pillar-to-pillar handoffs carry a typed envelope — turn_id, operator_id, charter_ref, task_tier (T0-T3), intent {type, sub_intents} from B1, context_envelope {my_own, worlds_best, peer_sutras_v2} from B2, decision_trail [{phase, factors, authority_verdict, output_ref}] from B3, outbound [{audience, channel, draft, sent_status, inbound_responses}] from B4, b5_writes {active_session_ref, provenance_row_ref, learned_pattern_updates} — Native enforces shape + authority on every handoff; the envelope must round-trip with zero data loss or HS-4 fires (§4.4). Draft run-flow narrative: steps execute in declared order (whether strictly one-at-a-time vs parallel-capable is not stated in the doc); each Step is an LLM call to the Host or a deterministic action wrapped in pre-declared-output + post-call check; hardstops HS-1..HS-8 can drop the runtime into PAUSED at any point; CSM sits alongside as the lifecycle ledger; operator feedback post-conclude feeds B5.d learned patterns.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-2-state-machine

Test-before-conclude gate (§4.5 accuracy primitive)master-spec"Native does NOT mark a task done before testing the output. Accuracy is a per-workflow KPI. Test happens BEFORE concluding, not after." Five steps: (1) pre-declare the test per task tier — T0 atomic: grep / file-exists / contract-match; T1 multi-action: step-by-step verify + final integration check; T2 iterative: operator-visible 1-min iteration verification; T3 Charter: milestone-gate verification at goal-transition points; (2) B3 generates output (auto-execute path); (3) run the pre-declared test — determinism: same input + same Charter → same expected output shape; (4) pass → conclude + write audit row + present; fail → loop to B3 retry (max N retries per Charter), after N failures escalate to operator via Authority gate; (5) operator feedback (drop / accept / refine) becomes test signal for B5.d learned patterns; test thresholds adapt over time per workflow. Rationale verbatim: "testing AFTER means the operator sees a failure and has to ask CoS to redo. Testing BEFORE means CoS catches its own failures and retries silently." The Accuracy KPI in §7 measures % of outputs that passed first-time-test vs needed retry.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-5-test-before-conclude
Resilience contract — hardstops HS-1..HS-8 + failure states PAUSED/ESCALATED/DEGRADED (§4.10)master-specThe hardstop catalog defines what the system does when it cannot continue safely; every hardstop produces (a) a 1-2 sentence operator message stating WHAT happened, (b) a recommended NEXT ACTION, (c) a link to the audit row — "the operator never debugs the system". HS-1 reflexive-check refuses self-modification without reflexive_check cleared; HS-2 aborts the turn at >25% governance token overhead; HS-3 denies cross-tenant access without delegates_to; HS-4 STOPS the system when DecisionProvenance is unwritable across all 3 channels ("no silent unaudited execution"); HS-5 pauses cutover into HITL when rollback itself fails; HS-6 suspends the session on 3+ governance-hook failures in one turn; HS-7 blocks shipments when the codex queue exceeds 20 items or 7-day staleness; HS-8 auto-rolls-back on canary regression. MECHANISM-PENDING callout (2026-05-13): HS-4..HS-8 are doctrine, mechanism pending — live detectors/executors do NOT yet exist in the shipped runtime; HS-1..HS-3 have at least partial enforcement via existing plugin hooks. §4.10.2 failure states extend the §4.2 happy path: PAUSED (hardstop fired or Charter Authority needs operator decision; exit via operator decide → ACTIVE or RETIRED), ESCALATED (B3 retry budget exhausted per §4.5, or Authority gate triggers ESCALATE class per ADR-018 contract 1; operator gets a B3a THINK 3-angle brainstorm + recommended angle), DEGRADED (connector half-fail / partial side-effect; exit via idempotent retry or manual log-write reconciliation).holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-10-1-hardstops

7d Lifecycle Orchestrator — 8-phase lifecycle as first-class Execution state (canon)native-canonSutra's 8-phase task lifecycle (OBJECTIVE → OBSERVE → SHAPE → PLAN → EXECUTE → MEASURE → OPERATIONALIZE → LEARN per D30a) becomes Native's first-class Execution-state phases: ExecutionResult gains a lifecycle_phase enum (per Q16 default 2026-05-09, founder-ratified — field-level extension, no new §2 primitive). Phase consumers: OBJECTIVE consumes B1 Intent; OBSERVE consumes 7a context-structuring; SHAPE consumes B2 Decomposition; PLAN consumes B11 PromptBuilder; EXECUTE runs the Workflow steps (host-LLM dispatch per §5.1); MEASURE evaluates B7 pre/post predicates; OPERATIONALIZE registers the Workflow for cadence-fire (§6.4 / ADR-017), gated v1 by per-cycle founder approval per Q13; LEARN closes the loop. Once operationalized, the Workflow re-fires per TriggerSpec on its cadence; each fire gets a fresh ExecutionResult sharing parent lifecycle lineage via parent_exec_id. Phase failure routes through canon on_failure machinery per §6.5 — the lifecycle does NOT auto-skip phases (no canon authorization for skip). Fully autonomous auto-run is out of scope v1 (Q13 defers to v2+ once a ≥30d clean-cadence trust signal lands); two lifecycles racing to operationalize the same Workflow are handled by the B13 ConcurrencyCoordinator.sutra/os/native/blocks/7d-lifecycle-orchestrator.md#1-line-summary

B13 Multi-Runtime Concurrency — ConcurrencyCoordinator with file-based locks (canon)native-canonTwo simultaneous runtime processes interact safely via a NEW ConcurrencyCoordinator (§12.17 row B13): file-based locks per Q33 default (matches the existing JSONL fsync pattern; portable + observable) on Artifact.id and ExecutionResult.id, with the lock table visible across processes. Flow: Runtime A acquires locks on the artifacts/executions it touches; Runtime B observes the lock and waits OR exits depending on policy (canon-silent on the wait-vs-exit default — runtime implementation choice); on release, B proceeds with a fresh read. Lock-file write failure (filesystem error) fires HS-4 per canon §6.9.4, fail-closed. Out of scope v1: DB-style serializable transactions (Q33 rejects for v1), optimistic concurrency with retry (v2 upgrade if perf signal lands), deadlock detection (explicitly v2 per §12.17), cross-process replay (§8 OS-3 deferred).sutra/os/native/blocks/B13-multi-runtime-concurrency.md#1-line-summary

Five disciplines converge on: a work system = contracted units + provably-safe composition + an append-only record from which the system can be rebuilt (replay) or rediscovered (mining); the full loop is design → execute → record → compare → redesign, with a formal result guarding each edge — "Native today runs design → execute → record". on_failure closed 5-set (verbatim, ADR-011)

PolicyEffect
rollbackCompensate steps in reverse; emit workflow_rollback_started → _complete or _partial
escalateEmit workflow_escalated; route to founder channel
pauseEmit step_paused; persist queue entry; resume on signal
abortEmit workflow_failed immediately
continueLog failure; advance to step[i+1]; set partial=true; do NOT abort

ExecutionResult states + terminal discipline (verbatim-derived, canon)

ItemRule
state enum{running, success, failed, awaiting_approval, paused, declared_gap} (§2.6 6-set)
I-4failure_reason === null IFF state ∈ {success, declared_gap}
I-14exactly one terminal event from {workflow_completed, workflow_failed, approval_requested}; rollback events are recovery, NOT terminal
I-15awaiting_approval requires ledger row at user-kit/pending-approvals/E-<id>.json
I-7 / I-8≥1 DecisionProvenance per consequential decision; tenant_context non-null cross-tenant (HS-3 otherwise)
timeoutsStep timeout_ms defaults: 60s claude --bare, ≥300s expected for complex codex (§6.7; RUN-I7)

Draft gap 2 (_block-system-of-process.html): the cited section-1-0-runtime text/SVG never explicitly states "only System of Process calls the LLM/Host" — the Host→Daemon edge is labeled "runs" (downward only); where is the SoP→Host call edge documented in the doc body, beyond the Step primitive implying it? (Manifest acceptance for this page expects the only-SoP-calls-Host locked-schema rule stated.)
Draft gap 3 (_block-system-of-process.html): boundary tension — section-4-0-daemon describes the WHOLE daemon, which per §1.0 also contains Orchestration as a sibling block; is the daemon substrate inside System of Process or shared with Orchestration, and where do Orchestration's own internals get documented?
Draft gap 4 (_block-system-of-process.html): §1.0 says "context-build" is second-level detail inside System of Process, but the cited §4 ranges only show the context_envelope arriving "from B2" — where is the SoP-side context-gathering/build mechanism specified at §4 level?
Draft gap 5 (_block-system-of-process.html): retry budget "max N retries per Charter" (§4.5) — N is undefined in the cited ranges; where does the per-Charter N get set and stored?
Draft gap 6 (_block-system-of-process.html) + master §4.10.1 callout: HS-4..HS-8 are MECHANISM-PENDING (detectors not in shipped runtime) — what does the runtime actually do today when those failure conditions occur, given the failure-state table assumes hardstops fire? Resolution path per §10.10 P0 #1: ship the missing detectors OR downgrade canon entries.
run.md canon gap (§8 OS-1): NativeEngine.on_host_llm_result(execId, stepIdx, r) is declared as the resumption callback after host-LLM returns, but the exact wire is open — RUN's contract for awaiting that callback is implementation-shaped, not canon-shaped.
execution-result.md canon gaps: resumption semantics for paused distinct from awaiting_approval are NOT fully specified in canon (runtime implementation choice); declared_gap has no event-type in §3.2's 26-event catalog (likely a typed DecisionProvenance row; future ADR may add an event).
B13 canon gaps: lock wait-vs-exit policy is canon-silent (gap per F2; runtime implementation choice); deadlock detection explicitly deferred to v2 — circular-wait behavior undefined in v1.
7d canon gaps: operator re-run of a single phase not specified (future ADR may codify replay-from-phase semantics); phase-skipping not specified (lifecycle is sequential v1).

ADR-022 context (named state of the world): the record today fails both litmus tests — not replayable and not regulated — "so the SoR is telemetry, not yet a system of record"; the loop closes only at mechanism #5; dual-lane retro review queued.

Four-horizon substrate B5.a–d — parallel time horizons, not a graduation pipelinesecond-order-draftThe four stores B5.a–d are parallel time horizons, not a graduation pipeline — the PERSISTED step writes output to B5.a/b/c/d simultaneously. B5.a (minutes–hours) is what's right-now in flight and backs N22 session-continuity with handoff of open threads. B5.b (hours–days) holds multi-task state for the 5 in-flight tasks across N companies, survives session restart, and backs N24; its engineering hard-problem (token budget allocation + multi-thread state machine) is deferred to Phase 2 design. B5.c (days–forever) accumulates the append-only DecisionProvenance rows forever and backs N#8, with acceptance: 100% of decisions carry a DecisionProvenance row and replay of any task end-to-end in ≤30s for a single-day window. B5.d (months–forever) distills learned patterns at k=4 emergence and backs N#5; operator standing prefs land here and future B2 reads them. B2 reads from this block at intake (ranked context retrieval) and B1 reads it for shorthand interpretation on future turns. The §1.0 diagram splits SoR contents as "own | external".holding/website/native/_block-system-of-record.html

Per-turn 5-artifact audit trail (§7.2)master-specEvery Native turn produces 5 stored artifacts: 1. intent record (B1), 2. context manifest with ranked artifact list + what was dropped (B2), 3. decision provenance with phases + factors + authority verdict + output (B3), 4. outbound log (B4, if comms triggered), 5. substrate write (B5: active-session ref + learned-pattern updates). Operator command "replay last Paisa turn" shows all 5 artifacts chronologically; "show me what changed in B5.d this week" returns a diff of learned patterns. Observability acceptance (v1): the 5-artifact trail is emitted per turn and HS-4 fires on any missing channel. The operator can request replay for any past turn within ≤30s for a single-day window. Drift signals are tracked weekly; breach surfaces to founder via Daily Pulse. B5 drift signals named in the draft: replay latency >60s OR any cross-tenant leak.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-7-2-audit

DecisionProvenance — typed audit row primitive (ADR-007 + canon)native-canonDecisionProvenance is Native's typed audit row for every consequential decision — Workflow-level, Step-level, Hook-level, Tenant-level, or Cutover-level; every Workflow/Execution/Hook emits ≥1 row per consequential decision (I-7). Fields per ADR-007/§2.9: id (uuid v4, unique), monotonic ts_ms, agent_identity chain (ADR-015, parent→child), policy_id + policy_version both non-empty (I-9 + I-17; empty either field is a HARD F-8 reject), scope ∈ {WORKFLOW, STEP, HOOK, TENANT, CUTOVER}, outcome ∈ {allow, deny, pause, escalate}, sanitized reason (no colons, no newlines — protects JSONL parsing), and data_refs[] each carrying authoritative_status per ADR-008. PolicyDispatcher.evaluate(scope, evidence) returns the row; UserKit.appendDecisionProvenance fsync-writes it to ~/.sutra-native/user-kit/decision-provenance.jsonl AND to the owning Tenant's audit_log_path when the decision touches a Tenant. Rows are inert immutable records — once persisted they are final, no state transitions. ADR-007's decision rationale: every consequential decision is replayable from a structured row, and policy version drift is detectable (same evidence + different policy_version → different outcome). ADR-007 rejected unstructured log append (loses policy version, fails F-8, blocks replay) and hook-scoped gate-log extension (Workflow/Step/Tenant/Cutover scopes need the same primitive uniformly).sutra/os/native/primitives/decision-provenance.md#purpose
sutra/os/native/primitives/decision-provenance.md#invariants-must-hold
sutra/os/native/primitives/decision-provenance.md#serialization-jsonl-row-shape
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-007-decision-provenance-schema.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-007-decision-provenance-schema.md#consequences
EngineEvent — append-only typed event log with exhaustive 26-type catalognative-canonEngineEvent is Native's append-only typed audit row — one JSONL line per event — forming the complete audit trail for Workflows, Executions, Triggers, approvals, rollbacks, pattern emergence, commitments, and tenant boundary violations. Every event carries event_type, ts_ms, execution_id?, payload, and agent_identity (per ADR-015). The 26-value event_type enum is exhaustive; new event-types require a NATIVE-ENGINE.md amendment + ADR, with mint-time reject on unknown types. Once persisted a row is immutable — no edit, no delete; replay derives full state from the log, and events MUST NOT be re-ordered by ts_ms after persistence. I-14: exactly ONE of {workflow_completed, workflow_failed, approval_requested} is emitted per Execution; recovery events are post-terminal transitions. Sinks: per-Execution log at ~/.sutra-native/user-kit/executions/E-<hash>.events.jsonl, global ~/.sutra-native/user-kit/events.jsonl (replay source), and the decision-provenance.jsonl subset. Consumers (replay, audit, telemetry-sink fan-out §5.6, H-Sutra event bus §5.3) read the events read-only.sutra/os/native/primitives/engine-event.md#purpose
sutra/os/native/primitives/engine-event.md#invariants-must-hold
sutra/os/native/primitives/engine-event.md#serialization-jsonl-row-shape
Telemetry sink — fsync'd JSONL append (ADR-013)adr-decisionNative engine MUST persist EngineEvent and DecisionProvenance as fsync'd JSONL append-only files; the H-Sutra event bus follows the same shape. One JSONL line per event/decision, UTF-8, no newline-in-field (sanitized at emit); fsync per append for durability — never lose a row on crash. Stderr beacon fallback when the primary path is unwritable, with dual fallback to /tmp (the HS-4 chain). The pattern detector (ADR-010) reads the log directly — no in-memory cache; restart-safe. Alternatives rejected: in-memory ring buffer (dies on daemon restart — codex P1.2 forced rejection) and SQLite/DB (adds dependency + schema migrations; JSONL won on append-only proof + zero-dep). Consequences: crash-safe and multi-process safe (POSIX append atomicity for short lines); no indexed query — replay is sequential scan, aggregation is downstream tooling; OTel production transport (OS-10) is the next-tier deferral, JSONL stays primary. The second-order draft labels this part "TELEMETRY SINK · ADR-013 · JSONL + fsync · no silent loss".sutra/os/decisions/ADR-013-telemetry-sink-fsync-jsonl.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-013-telemetry-sink-fsync-jsonl.md#consequences
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-013-telemetry-sink-fsync-jsonl.md#context
holding/website/native/_block-system-of-record.html
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-12-1-adr-table
HS-4 — DecisionProvenance log unwritable across all 3 channelsnative-canonHS-4 fires when a consequential decision must emit a DecisionProvenance row (per I-7) and the primary JSONL append fails AND the stderr beacon fallback fails AND the /tmp dual-fallback fails — all 3 channels simultaneously unavailable. Fail-mode: block all governance hooks; a stderr beacon is emitted as the surface-of-last-resort even when the audit log itself is unwritable. Per I-7 + I-9 + I-17, governance cannot proceed without a writable provenance surface — audit-trail integrity is preserved by blocking, not by writing without trace. Recovery: operator observes the beacon and restores at least one of the 3 channels before governance hooks resume. Operator-visible behavior per master §4.10.1: system STOPS and surfaces "cannot record this decision; halting until audit recovers" — no silent unaudited execution. The second-order draft and B5 acceptance add: HS-4 fires on triple-channel write failure and on any silent overwrite (version-bump-less replace per D.12 acceptance).sutra/os/native/hardstops/HS-4-audit-unwritable.md#context-when-this-fires
sutra/os/native/hardstops/HS-4-audit-unwritable.md#decision-fail-mode
sutra/os/native/hardstops/HS-4-audit-unwritable.md#recovery-path

AUDIT surface — persistence + replay contractnative-canonEvery state transition across every surface emits exactly one typed EngineEvent + (for consequential decisions) one DecisionProvenance row; AUDIT is the persistence + replay surface for those rows. AUDIT is not directly invoked — every other surface (ROUTE/RUN/GATE/EMERGE/TENANT) calls UserKit.appendDecisionProvenance(dp) or emits EngineEvent rows that AUDIT persists. Invariants AUDIT-I1..I8: ≥1 EngineEvent per transition (append-only); exactly one DecisionProvenance per consequential decision; monotonic ts_ms; sanitized reason; durability via fsync per append + 3-channel fallback + stderr beacon last-resort; fail-CLOSED on audit, never fail-open (HS-4); non-empty policy_id + policy_version; chain-shaped agent_identity per ADR-015. AUDIT persists ALL 26 EngineEvent types of §3.2. Replay per §6.1: every consequential decision is rebuildable from agent_identity + policy_id + policy_version + data_refs + outcome. Canon gaps the surface itself names: cross-process replay deferred per §8 OS-3, and an "audit_log_write_failed" event is not in §3.2 — HS-4 uses stderr beacon, not an EngineEvent.sutra/os/native/surfaces/audit.md#purpose

Versioned artifact store — D.12 lifecycle integritymaster-specAppend, don't overwrite: every change adds a version (semver or date-stamped); the prior version stays accessible; no silent rewrites. Notify-on-change: when an artifact shared with audience X changes, audience X is notified by §5 Connector (Slack/email per Charter) — within 60s per acceptance. Reference-with-version: every cross-reference embeds the version it points to (e.g. paisa-Q3-onepager@v3) and resolves to that exact version even after newer versions ship. Pristine boundaries: metadata (owner, version, source-of-truth status) travels with the artifact, not in a sidecar that can desync. Audit-traceable: every artifact write produces a DecisionProvenance row linking creator-Charter + reason + diff-from-prior. Acceptance criteria (v1): 100% of Native-produced artifacts carry version + owner + Charter-ref metadata; zero silent overwrites (HS-4 fires on any write that replaces without version-bump); broken references surface to operator, never silently 404.holding/website/native/master/index.html#d12-artifact-governance

Personalization memory — skill modes (E.1), charter prefs (E.2), memory layers (E.3), forget rules (E.4)second-order-draftMemory layers (E.3) are five layers with distinct lifespans: working memory (session, handoff if thread open); named entities (until forget or 90-day inactive + confirmed archive); trusted names (durable, change needs explicit confirmation); prior intents (rolling, older entries summarize); ratified directives (until revoked). Forget rules (E.4): one-time sensitive content, expired time-bounded charters, and anything the operator asks to forget — honored immediately and visibly confirmed. Skill modes (E.1) are a per-domain skill-mode map (SM1 novice / SM2 intermediate / SM3 expert) for how Native talks to this operator — inferred from phrasing/corrections or set explicitly; per-domain, not global; a separate mechanism from the E.2 charter. Charter & declared preferences (E.2) capture what the operator explicitly told Native about how to be served — cadence, hands-off level per ask class, communication style, channel defaults, people & relationships — durable until revoked. The operator curation surface answers "what do you remember about X?" with a structured view of entities, prior intents, standing directives, and charter entries touching X. Operator forget/correct/pin-as-durable commands enter via the UI→Host→Daemon runtime path.holding/website/native/_block-system-of-record.html

Authoritative-vs-advisory state on every DataRef (ADR-008)adr-decisionNative engine MUST require every DataRef to carry an explicit authoritative_status ∈ {authoritative, advisory}. Readers honor authoritative over advisory at lookup time — declarative precedence, no convention. Workflow inputs[] and outputs[] tag each entry, enforced at primitive-mint via ajv schema validation. DecisionProvenance.data_refs[] carries the same field — every audit row records which version of which surface was load-bearing (markdown vs code per row). Drift detection: weekly cadence reports the authoritative-vs-advisory diff for each known artifact pair, making drift a measurable signal instead of a surprise. Alternatives rejected: implicit "code wins" (newly-captured markdown directions must be load-bearing), implicit "markdown wins" (runtime decisions are the live contract), and single-source-of-truth collapse (both surfaces serve different audiences — founder reads markdown, daemon reads code).sutra/os/decisions/ADR-008-markdown-vs-code-authoritative-state.md#decision

Shipped production — coverage event stream as a Native SoR table (engine/coverage)shipped-productionProduction ships a live five-layer process-coverage system: COVERAGE-ENGINE.md depth-indexed expected checklist (D1=4, D2=7, D3=14, D4=19, D5=24 method IDs) with the coverage-log.jsonl row schema {task, depth, method, ts, evidence, skipped_reason, override} and an evidence-specificity rule ('if your evidence could describe any task, it's not evidence'); os/method-registry.jsonl encoding all 26 methods (id/category/min_depth/required_at/phase/conditional); writers log-coverage.sh (HARD-rejects any runtime skip attempt — 5th arg → exit 2, per D31 'clients execute, never skip') and auto-coverage.sh (declarative path/command → method mapping with per-turn dedupe, the dominant production writer); a Stop-time coverage-gate computing expected = enabled_methods ∩ required_at[depth]; and observability-synthesis.sh deriving coverage-fires-per-commit with RED classification when commits>0 but fires=0. The dossier's redo recipe: port os/method-registry.jsonl verbatim as a Native SoR table — it IS the contract — and reimplement the writer as EngineEvent emission in the daemon, so coverage rows become Execution-linked events instead of grep-appended JSONL. Gate and report become queries over those events, keeping SOFT-first with codified HARD triggers. The dossier's stated gap: Native names the Coverage Engine only in the §11.3 engines listing and lacks all five shipped layers.holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#engine/coverage
Shipped production — latency rows into the ADR-013 telemetry sink (charter/speed)shipped-productionThe SPEED charter ships the Iron Rule ('No Speed improvement ships without a cited time-sink baseline from this charter's data'), 8 KPI formulas with Warn/Breach thresholds (governance overhead % target <15%, breach ≥30% auto-becomes the #1 cut), the full phase taxonomy tree with an unambiguous start/end boundary-event table per phase, 8 collector invariants (I1: sum(phase_ms) within ±2% of wall), and the canonical LATENCY-LOG.jsonl row schema. The W1 collector latency-collector.sh appends one row per task (wall_ms, tool/result/thinking/subagent counts, full token usage incl. cache_read/cache_creation, partial flag), always exit 0. Operational state: the pipeline is DORMANT — dispatcher-stop.sh invokes a holding copy that no longer exists on disk and LATENCY-LOG.jsonl is frozen at 10 rows from 2026-04-20. The dossier's redo recipe: production's failure mode argues for emitting latency rows from Native's own engine events into the ADR-013 fsync-JSONL telemetry sink, so measurement cannot silently decouple from execution; port the taxonomy, formulas, invariants and row schema verbatim as the measured-universe IP. Shipped production — fleet telemetry manifest queried over the telemetry SoR (cap/100)shipped-productionProduction ships fleet.sh — the 'who is running the plugin, right now' dashboard — parsing every clients/<install_id>/manifest.json in the sutra-data cache into rows {install_id, github_login, git_user_name, os_pretty, arch, tz, project_name, push_count, sutra_version, last_seen, age_days, has_identity}. Output: 5 aggregates (total installs, identified count/%, unique github logins, unique humans, total pushes) plus a per-install table sorted last_seen descending; --fresh restricts to installs seen within 7 days; an empty cache exits 1 with clone instructions. Privacy default: since plugin v2.0.0 outbound telemetry is DISABLED by default per PRIVACY.md.
