▸ authority-tenancy — engineering detail

MechanismKindWhat exists (compiled — zero invention)Sources
The wrap — gates every access (§1.0), drawn as border not pipelinesecond-order-draft§1.0 draws Authority + Tenancy as a dashed wrap around the entire runtime ("AUTHORITY + TENANCY · gates every access") — a cross-cutting envelope, not an in-line stage on any single edge. Internal flow per the second-order draft: every access entering the wrap is classified by the D.6 six-dimension Authority Model, then run through the F.5 ask-vs-act cascade; any ASK outcome is realized by the approval-gate primitive (ADR-009), which surfaces the prompt up through UI carrying its F.2 reversibility tag, while the gated workflow inside System of Process holds in PAUSED or ESCALATED until the operator's decision flows back down. In parallel, the tenancy half checks every System-of-Record touch against the ADR-006 Domain field, with HS-3 default-denying cross-tenant access lacking an explicit delegates_to edge; denials surface via the hardstop UX contract (WHAT + next action + audit-row link). Operator responses ("grant always", dial up/down) feed the F.4 trust ladder, which shifts future cascade outcomes from ask toward auto over rungs 1-4. Every decision through the gate writes a DecisionProvenance row, which the F.3 replay surface reads back on demand, and the §1.5.1 guard tooling enforces the same contract at action-time as HARD/SOFT blocks.holding/website/native/_block-authority-tenancy.html
holding/website/native/master/index.html#so-authority-tenancy
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-1-0-runtime
Authority Model — six gate dimensions (D.6)master-specSix dimensions gate Native's auto-execute vs ask-operator decisions. By domain: Native acts in one company's domain but not another (per Charter). By tool: Native can call Slack but not bank-API without auth. By stakeholder: Native emails team but escalates board/regulator. By dollar threshold: ≤$X auto, >$X ask. By reversibility: reversible auto, irreversible ask. By confidence level: high-confidence auto, low-confidence ask. Applies cross-pillar — most heavily in B3 (action gate) and B4 (delegation through Native to humans).holding/website/native/master/index.html#d6-authority
holding/website/native/_block-authority-tenancy.html
Ask-vs-act decision cascade (F.1 + F.5)second-order-draftOrdered 4-question cascade per action. Irreversible? → ASK multi-step confirm ("type go" two-step). High-risk (spend/send/share/commit touching people or money)? → ASK one-tap confirm ("confirm — send / edit / cancel"). New authority (no prior permission template)? → ASK "grant once / grant always / not now". First time on channel/contact? → ASK preview-before-send ("preview before I send?"). All no → ACT, report after. The cascade's ASK outcomes route to the approval-gate primitive, and GATE/ESCALATE outcomes hold the workflow in the §4.10.2 failure states.holding/website/native/_block-authority-tenancy.html
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-2-f-6-trust-authority
holding/website/native/master/index.html#so-authority-tenancy
Reversibility tags (F.2)second-order-draftEvery action carries a visible reversibility tag with three values. Reversible: undo window — "say undo anytime". Reversible with effort: retract/re-issue/re-pay — doable, not free. Not reversible: confirming locks it in. The tag is visible on every action and appears on every approval prompt surfaced up through UI.holding/website/native/_block-authority-tenancy.html
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-2-f-6-trust-authority
Trust ladder (F.4) — autonomy earned over timesecond-order-draftFour rungs: Rung 1 (day 0-14) ask before every external action; Rung 2 (15-45) auto-act on repeated patterns, ask on novel; Rung 3 (46-90) auto-act on routines, ask only high-risk/irreversible; Rung 4 (90+) runs standing routines and surfaces what it did. The operator can dial up/down explicitly at any rung. "Grant always" responses and dial moves feed the ladder, which shifts future cascade outcomes from ask toward auto.holding/website/native/_block-authority-tenancy.html
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-2-f-6-trust-authority
Approval gate — first-class primitive (ADR-009 + canon Approval + GATE surface)native-canonNative engine MUST add requires_approval: bool at both Workflow and WorkflowStep levels, with the typed event triplet approval_requested → approval_granted | approval_denied (ADR-009; approval is a first-class primitive, not a hook). Reaching a requires_approval=true step: LiteExecutor persists a ledger row at user-kit/pending-approvals/E-<id>.json carrying {workflow_id, step_index, ts_ms, prompt_summary} (I-15 — hardstop on absence), emits approval_requested (#15, terminal per I-14), and transitions the Execution to awaiting_approval — fail-CLOSED, pausing BEFORE dispatching the action. Founder utterance approve E-<id> → NativeEngine.resumeApproved(execId) continues at step_index+1 and emits approval_granted (#16); reject E-<id> <reason> emits approval_denied (#17) and routes per Workflow.failure_policy to workflow_failed. Idempotent re-fire on a resolved approval emits approval_already_handled (#18) carrying prior_outcome — resolution is one-shot, and ledger rows are removed on first resolution. The daemon is detached from stdin, so inline Y/N prompts are impossible — approval is utterance-driven from the founder's interactive session, and the persistent ledger row survives daemon restart. v1 ships single-founder approval (Q5 ratified 2026-05-09); multi-party quorum is deferred to v2+ per OS-15. approve P-<id> approves a proposed Workflow and routes to EMERGE (ADR-010), NOT GATE — a documented cross-surface boundary.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-009-approval-gate-primitive.md#decision

Tenant primitive + tenant isolation via Domain.tenant_id (ADR-006)native-canonThe Tenant primitive is Native's isolation boundary: every Domain is owned by exactly one Tenant (I-13 — required field, no nullable). Tenant fields: content-addressed id (T-hash), non-empty name, isolation_contract (filesystem + capability isolation declaration), parent_tenant_id (null for root), and an absolute audit_log_path to the Tenant's DecisionProvenance JSONL. Execution.tenant_context carries {tenant_id}, and TenantIsolation.assertCrossTenantAllowed(src, dst, op) is called unconditionally when a step touches a different tenant. Cross-tenant ops without an explicit delegates_to edge fail-closed and emit tenant_boundary_violation (#26; HS-3, I-8). ADR-006 rejected reusing Cohort (a routing/targeting primitive — a Workflow can target multiple Cohorts; a Domain belongs to exactly one Tenant) and relying solely on the D33 settings.json firewall (intent-level, not capability-enforced); D33 stays as defense-in-depth. Consequences: the tenant boundary is a typed schema field checked at primitive-mint AND execution; the audit trail records every cross-tenant attempt; migration cost — every existing Domain must be assigned a tenant_id (no default); hierarchical/nested tenancy is declared in schema but execution semantics are v1.x backlog.sutra/os/native/primitives/tenant.md#purpose

HS-3 — tenant boundary cross without TenantDelegationnative-canonHS-3 fires when an Execution attempts to cross a Tenant boundary without an explicit delegates_to edge: the Execution carries tenant_context.tenant_id, a step targets a resource whose effective tenant differs, and no delegates_to edge authorizes the cross (I-8). Fail-mode: block + log + escalate — the op is blocked at TenantIsolation.assertCrossTenantAllowed, a tenant_boundary_violation event (#26) emits carrying src_tenant/dst_tenant/op, and a DecisionProvenance row is logged with policy_id='HS-3'. Fail-closed when workflow.custody_owner !== null AND tenant_context_id is undefined. Recovery requires the operator to authorize the cross via an explicit delegates_to edge. Operator-visible behavior per master §4.10.1: cross-company action DENIED, with the prompt "this action would touch <other-company>; explicit delegates_to authorization required". Master's MECHANISM-PENDING callout records that HS-3 has only partial enforcement via existing plugin hooks today.sutra/os/native/hardstops/HS-3-tenant-boundary.md#context-when-this-fires

TENANT surface — isolated audit log + ACL per companynative-canonTENANT provides each Tenant with an isolated audit log + ACL — cross-Tenant operations require explicit delegation, every Tenant's DecisionProvenance is private by default, and cross-Tenant leakage is fail-CLOSED. It is consulted on every Execution dispatch and every cross-Tenant operation; it is not a CLI surface but is enforced via TenantIsolation.assertCrossTenantAllowed, which throws on deny. Invariants: every Domain has a non-null tenant_id (TENANT-I1); every Tenant has non-empty name + absolute audit_log_path (TENANT-I2); cross-Tenant ops require TenantDelegation, else throw + #26 + HS-3 (TENANT-I3); fail-CLOSED when custody_owner !== null AND tenant_context.tenant_id undefined (TENANT-I4); each Tenant's DecisionProvenance JSONL is private absent explicit delegation (TENANT-I5); HS-3 fires block+log+escalate on any violation (TENANT-I6). Operator-visible touchpoints: Tenant mint with declared audit_log_path, `sutra-native tenant list` (§3.3), and the violation path. Upstream surfaces ROUTE/RUN/GATE/EMERGE all respect Tenant scope; downstream, AUDIT persists to the per-Tenant log and HS-3 handles violation escalation.sutra/os/native/surfaces/tenant.md#purpose

Hardstop UX contract + HS-1 reflexive checkmaster-specEvery hardstop produces (a) a 1-2 sentence operator-facing message stating WHAT happened, (b) a recommended NEXT ACTION (decide / authorize / wait / investigate), and (c) a link to the audit row for the event; the operator never has to read engineer-level stack traces — the system translates. HS-1 (reflexive check) fires when a Workflow with modifies_sutra=true attempts to write Sutra core paths (plugin hooks/skills, charter files) without the reflexive_check Constraint cleared. Fail-mode: HARD reject + Founder ASK gate — LiteExecutor rejects the step dispatch before any host-LLM call fires, a policy_decision event emits with outcome='deny' and policy_id='HS-1', a DecisionProvenance row references the F-7 violation, and the daemon surfaces the rejected step + provenance to the founder via the approval primitive (ADR-009). The Workflow does NOT auto-retry; the founder must clear the reflexive_check Constraint via explicit approval before resumption. Operator-visible per master: system REFUSES to self-modify and surfaces "this change touches Sutra's own rules — please ratify directly".holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-10-1-hardstops

Authority-driven failure states — PAUSED / ESCALATED (§4.10.2)master-specPAUSED is entered when a hardstop fires (HS-N) OR Charter Authority requires an explicit operator decision (per §D.6); the operator sees "task paused at step X — decision needed" with context + recommended next action; exit path: operator decides → ACTIVE (resume) OR RETIRED (cancel). ESCALATED is entered when the B3 retry budget exhausts (per §4.5 test-before-conclude) OR the Authority gate triggers the ESCALATE class (per ADR-018 contract 1's AUTO/GATE/ESCALATE classification); the operator sees "auto-retry exhausted; your judgment needed" with a B3a THINK 3-angle brainstorm + recommended angle; exit path: operator confirms direction → ACTIVE (B3b auto-executes the chosen path) OR RETIRED. These failure states extend the §4.2 happy-path lifecycle (PROPOSED → ACTIVE → PERSISTED → RETIRED). The risk-owner map assigns R4 operator review fatigue to §D.6 Authority Model trust signal + ADR-018 contract 1, and R3 cross-tenant leakage to §4.10 HS-3 + ADR-006.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-10-2-failure-states
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-10-3-risk-owners

Guard tooling — §1.5.1 governance machinery (projection; primary home is governance band)master-specLive action-time guards enforce the authority + audit + intervention contract as HARD/SOFT blocks: when a guard fires HARD, the operator sees a boxed BLOCKED message with the rule cited + override path (typically {D_TAG}_ACK=1 {D_TAG}_ACK_REASON='<why>' <cmd> — honor-system at env-var level, audit-logged); when SOFT, the operator sees an advisory and the tool call proceeds. Tenancy-relevant rows in the §1.5.1 table: the external-client repo firewall (blocks cross-repo access from holding workspace into external client trees per D33, HARD) and god-mode (time-boxed cross-company deployment access, CEO-of-Asawa only, 2h auto-deactivate, logged to .enforcement/god-mode.log, opt-in HARD scope). The §1.5 Governance Machinery row states every decision carries a provenance row and cross-tenant is default-deny. Per the manifest, this page draws the wrap relationship to the governance band as a projection — content is not duplicated; §1.5.1 machinery's primary home is governance/ (codex MODIFY-2).holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-1-5-1-governance-machinery

Shipped production — security charter + boundary/elevation primitives (charter/security)shipped-productionProduction ships SECURITY.md charter v1.0.0: a 3-threat-surface model (Surface 1 Asawa IP = confidentiality; Surface 2 fleet integrity = "one compromised push → all tenants have arbitrary code execution"; Surface 3 tenant authenticity), 8 ordered principles including fail-closed and identity boundaries ("'You're authenticated as X, I can act as X' patterns are banned"), a T0-T4 tiered access contract, and a 14-primitive inventory (8 shipped, 6 needed: signed releases, SHA-pinned submodules, god-mode MFA, update-consent, SBOM cadence, audit rollup). Shipped enforcement: enforce-boundaries.sh (PreToolUse, fail-closed role resolution, exit 2 blocks cross-submodule edits, god-mode file bypass) and god-mode.sh (password-gated 2h TTL elevation, every activate/deactivate/expiry appended to .enforcement/god-mode.log — 33 rows live). Behavior contract: any Write/Edit from a non-matching role into another submodule exits 2 with unresolvable role failing CLOSED; cross-company writes from holding succeed only inside the god-mode TTL window. The dossier's redo recipe: re-implement the tenant/role boundary as a Native hardstop keyed on ADR-006's domain field instead of bash path-matching, and god-mode as a first-class time-boxed authority-elevation primitive (TTL + append-only audit) rather than a marker file; the identity-leak defense should become enforced policy, not injected context. The dossier's stated gap: Native has the pieces piecemeal (HS-3, ADR-006, god-mode time-box) but lacks the charter's consolidating layer — the 3-surface model, the T0-T4 contract, the failure-mode→primitive map, and KR1-KR7 measurement; HLD/07 Security remains planned-only (T-G8-08 TODO).holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#charter/security
Shipped production — competing-priorities escalation trigger (dir/D20)shipped-productionD20 doctrine: "When high-priority items compete, do NOT pick autonomously. Present the tension — what competes, why each matters, what's at stake — and let the founder decide. Applies at every level: company, initiative, task." Enforcement is HARD behavioral — "agent must ask, not decide" — with AskUserQuestion as the mechanism plus the boxed-decision presentation contract; there is no dedicated hook file by design. Behavior contract: when 2+ high-priority items compete for the same resources, the system presents the tension as an interactive boxed question and blocks on the operator's choice, never resolving the conflict autonomously. The dossier's redo recipe: Native already has the machinery (ADR-009 approval-gate primitive, ESCALATED lifecycle state, M5 approval-ask card) — add the missing trigger class: when the scheduler/planner detects 2+ queued items with conflicting resource claims at comparable priority, transition to ESCALATED and emit an approval-ask card framed as present-tension-with-options, with resolution requiring an explicit operator selection event before either item proceeds. Maps to requirement N32.holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#dir/D20
Shipped production — conflict-hold semantics (dir/D23)shipped-productionD23 doctrine: "When a change conflicts with existing principles/directions, present the conflict and require explicit founder approval via AskUserQuestion. Without approval, the system holds. The OS challenges, the founder rules." Enforcement: "HARD — system holds until founder approves via interactive prompt. No silent overrides"; absence of approval means no-op, never best-effort. A concrete shipped instance is the operator-awareness guard: edits to charters/PERMISSIONS Tier 3/FOUNDER-DIRECTIONS/D-tagged files by a non-founder git identity are treated as REQUIRES-FOUNDER-APPROVAL with a boxed confirmation and no auto-execution. The dossier's redo recipe: implement as a conflict-detection pass over Native's direction/principle registry before any mutating action, feeding ADR-009's gate primitive in hold mode; semantics to port: fail-closed hold-by-default, the conflict statement must name the specific conflicting principle, and approval is recorded with provenance (ADR-007 schema). The pushback register belongs in doctrine text, not just gate mechanics. Maps to requirement N34.holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#dir/D23
Shipped production — keys-in-env-vars boundary block (proto/004 → canon HS-9)shipped-productionPROTO-004 is ACTIVE, HARD, enforced at two layers: plugin keys-in-env-vars.sh (PreToolUse on Write|Edit|MultiEdit) scans INCOMING content for provider-shaped key regexes (sk-…, AKIA…, ghp_…, ghs_…, xoxb-…, ya29.…, AIza…, BEGIN PRIVATE KEY — OWASP/git-secrets sourced) and exit-2 BLOCKS with a JSONL audit row, exempting env-type files, privacy-scrub libs, and binaries, with PROTO004_ACK=1 + reason as the audited override and D32 default-OFF opt-in; the Asawa dispatcher Check 5 (always-on) blocks assignment-shaped secrets, with only secret-INTRODUCING edits blocked — remediation edits pass. Canon already carries the lesson-port as HS-9 (DRAFT v1, authored 2026-06-12): secrets live only in env vars/env-type files, any artifact write introducing a key-shaped string is denied at the boundary BEFORE the file is touched, and out-of-repo 0600 stores (~/.sutra-connectors/oauth/ precedent) are a second, separately-governed sanctioned pattern gated by ADR-003 (deepseek MODIFY absorbed 2026-06-12). HS-9 declares the Native kill-switch key SUTRA_HS9_KEYS_DISABLED per the B10 K4 registry mandate. Recovery: move the secret to an env var or sanctioned store and re-issue; on a historical leak, rotate the credential first — rotation is the recovery, history rewriting is not. The dossier's stated gap: Native names the hook in §4.11.A but states no key-handling invariant; §5.1 stores connector tokens in oauth JSON files with no rule explaining why that is allowed.holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#proto/004

Shipped production — role-scoped boundary confinement for the tenancy wrap (hier/l4 + hier/l5)shipped-productionProduction confines a company-{name} session role to its own tree via enforce-boundaries.sh: edits outside the role's repo exit 2, and role resolution fails CLOSED (exit 2) when the role cannot be determined; the D33 firewall keeps external clients as standalone repos with the plugin as the only channel. L4 blast radius ("only this company") is enforced negatively and positively: cascade-check.sh does NOT match company os/ paths (no fleet-cascade obligation), and a company-scoped role cannot write outside its own tree. L5 (application code) carries zero upward blast: edits trigger no cascade obligation, and problems discovered in code reach higher tiers only through the feedback channel (feedback-to-sutra/ then PROTO-024 gh-issues fan-in), never by directly modifying a higher level. Both dossiers' redo recipes name this subsystem as the port target: "port enforce-boundaries.sh role confinement to Native's tenancy wrap" (hier/l4) and "Port enforce-boundaries.sh's role table + fail-closed resolution into Native's tenancy wrap" (hier/l5), declaring the single-company blast radius in canon and keeping deployment-scope paths exempt from any cascade gate.holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#hier/l4

D.6 Authority Model dimensions (verbatim from master)

DimensionWhat it gates
by domainNative acts in one company's domain but not another (per Charter)
by toolNative can call Slack but not bank-API without auth
by stakeholderNative emails team but escalates board/regulator
by dollar threshold≤$X auto; >$X ask
by reversibilityreversible auto; irreversible ask
by confidence levelhigh-confidence auto; low-confidence ask

Approval ledger + utterance grammar (canon §6.6 / §3.4 / ADR-009)

ElementContract
Ledger rowuser-kit/pending-approvals/E-<id>.json carrying {execution_id, workflow_id, step_index, ts_ms, prompt_summary} (I-15; removed on first resolution)
approve E-<id>Router parses → approval_granted (#16) → NativeEngine.resumeApproved(execId) → continue at step_index+1
reject E-<id> <reason>approval_denied (#17) → routes per Workflow.failure_policy → workflow_failed (failure_reason non-null per I-4)
approve P-<id>Proposed-Workflow approval — routes to EMERGE (ADR-010), NOT GATE
Re-fire on resolvedapproval_already_handled (#18) carrying prior_outcome — resolution is one-shot
Terminal semanticsapproval_requested (#15) is one of three I-14 terminals; Execution state → awaiting_approval

RungWindowBehavior
1day 0-14ask before every external action
2day 15-45auto-act on repeated patterns, ask on novel
3day 46-90auto-act on routines, ask only high-risk/irreversible
4day 90+runs standing routines, surfaces what it did
any—operator can dial up/down explicitly at any rung

§1.0 placement — External World as runtime counterpart block (context + effects edge)master-specPer the §1.0 system architecture, the System of Process runs the process (this is the runtime), gathering context from the System of Record + the External World and writing output back to the System of Record; Authority + Tenancy gates every access. In the §1.0 diagram the External World box is labeled 'services · people' and its edge to System of Process carries 'context + effects' (the System of Record edge carries 'context + output'). §1.0 also states that second-level detail (context-build · verify · connectors) lives inside System of Process — see §4. ADR-021 records the locked first-layer schema as 8 blocks: UI · Host (Claude CLI) · Orchestration · System of Process · System of Record · Authority+Tenancy · Compute · External World.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-1-0-runtime
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-021-sutra-plugin-host-residency.md#context
Second-order internal flow — parallel counterparties, two one-way Connector lanessecond-order-draftThe parts of the External World do not connect to each other — they are parallel counterparties, each reached over its own Connector channel. Traffic flows in two one-way lanes through the Connector transport (§5.2): on the outbound lane, System of Process output (B3's decision, B4's draft) passes the Authority gate, becomes a typed envelope with retry logic, and lands on a service endpoint (Slack, email, web, 3rd-party tool) or, through Slack/email, on a human stakeholder. On the inbound lane, a human's reply (e.g. the board chair's Slack response) is identity-verified, wrapped into a typed envelope, and routed back to B3 as the next action — so people and services only 'meet' inside System of Process, never inside the External World block. Peer Sutras have only a log-only A2A stub edge in v1 (no real transport), and browser/phone counterparts have no live edge at all. At the §1.0 altitude the whole block collapses to a single bidirectional 'context + effects' edge with System of Process, with Authority + Tenancy wrapping every crossing. The draft's System of Process neighbor stub states 'Connector lanes live here (§5.2)' — matching the manifest acceptance line 'Connector lanes live in SoP; External World owns counterparties' (codex MODIFY-4), with platform/connector.html cited for transport.holding/website/native/_block-external-world.html (Internal flow + SoP neighbor stub)
holding/website/native/master/index.html#so-external-world
holding/website/native/manifest.json (platform-external-world acceptance row)
Counterparty inventory — four internal partssecond-order-draftThe draft names four internal parts. People (operator's stakeholders): humans Native talks to on the operator's behalf — board / founder / team / client / vendor, each with audience-calibrated voice per the CX Bar overlay; the doc's worked example is a board chair receiving an audience-calibrated Slack DM and replying inbound ('got it; let's discuss Friday'). External services: service endpoints reached via Connector transport — Slack (in + out, Live), Email via Resend (out, Live — daily pulse + notifications), Web fetch/search (out, Live — feeds B2.b world's-best context), 3rd-party tools (out, pluggable via Composio with per-tool Charter scoping); §5.2 names them collectively 'THE WORLD (Slack · email · web · 3rd-pty)'. Peer Sutras: other operators' Native instances; v1 status is log-only A2A stub, no real transport; full peer-Native A2A is v2+, gated per §1.4.5 activation map. Deferred-channel counterparts: endpoints listed in the v1 inventory but not reachable yet — Browser co-work session (in + out, Charter TODO-005, deferred to v1.x, spec pending) and Phone voice + SMS humans (not planned v1, v2+ per §1.4.5 phone-connector activation gate).holding/website/native/_block-external-world.html (Internal parts table)

Neighbor contracts — SoP edges + Authority/Tenancy wrap on every crossingsecond-order-draftThe contracts table declares four neighbor edges. System of Process (bidirectional): 'context + effects' per the §1.0 edge label — SoP gathers context FROM the External World and pushes effects INTO it; this is one of the two external edges of the runtime (the other being System of Record, which gets 'context + output'). System of Process (in): outbound sends via the Connector outbound lane (B3 → B4) — audience-calibrated draft converted to a typed envelope, Authority-gated at every send, retry logic on transient fail, DecisionProvenance row per send; permanent failures surface to operator, no silent loss. System of Process (out): inbound receives via the Connector inbound lane (B4 → B3) — identity-verified, payload converted to a typed envelope, routed to B3 as next action; inbound from unverified identity logs + drops and never enters B3; inbound responses re-enter the loop via B3 and prior comms become B2 future context. Authority + Tenancy (gates): every outbound call passes the Authority gate with default-deny on missing scope; per-tool Charter scoping enforced (e.g. 'Slack:#paisa-team only', not 'Slack:*'); B4 adds per-stakeholder + tool + $-threshold gating on who Native may talk to.holding/website/native/_block-external-world.html (Contracts — neighbor edges table)

▸ connector — engineering detail

MechanismKindWhat exists (compiled — zero invention)Sources
§5 layer contract — purpose, owns, depends-on, does-not-ownmaster-spec§5 Connector is the transport layer: purpose is to move messages + tool calls between Native (§4) and the world outside (humans, services, peer Sutras). It owns Slack · email · web · browser · phone · 3rd-party tool integrations · inbound/outbound message handling · retry semantics. It depends on the D.6 Authority gate (at every send) and §7 Observability (for delivery health). It does NOT own what to say (B3), decision logic (B3), Charter scope (§4), or audience selection (B4 + Authority). The §1.2 layer boundary table repeats the contract — §5 owns 'Slack/email/web/browser/phone transport; 3rd-party tool integration; inbound/outbound message handling', depends on '§4 Authority gate at every send', does not own 'Decision logic; charter scope; what to say' — and records that §4 Native Platform depends on '§5 Connector for any out-of-process tool call' while §2 CoS depends on '§5 Connector transport'.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-5-connector
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-1-2-boundary
§5.2 inbound/outbound flow semanticsmaster-specThe §5.2 flow diagram splits Connector traffic into an outbound lane (B3 → B4) and an inbound lane (B4 → B3) on either side of §4 Native Platform. The outbound Connector applies: Authority gate, draft → typed envelope, retry logic. The inbound Connector applies: identity verify, payload → typed envelope, route to B3. Both lanes terminate at 'THE WORLD (Slack · email · web · 3rd-pty)'. The diagram caption fixes the per-message contract: every send is Authority-gated, writes a DecisionProvenance row, and retries on transient fail; every receive is identity-verified, typed-envelope wrapped, and routed to B3 as the next action.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-5-2-flow
§5.3 acceptance criteria (Connector v1)master-specFour acceptance criteria are stated for Connector v1. Every outbound call passes through the Authority gate; default-deny on missing scope. Inbound from unverified identity logs + drops, and doesn't enter B3. Transient failures retry; permanent failures surface to the operator (no silent drop). Per-tool scoping is enforced via Charter (e.g. 'Slack:#paisa-team only' not 'Slack:*').holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-5-3-acceptance
B17 — every external tool is a typed Connector (canon spec)native-canonB17 canon: every external tool (Slack, Email, web, etc.) is a typed Connector — inbound messages become Artifacts in the catalog; outbound messages emit DecisionProvenance + Artifact rows; Workflow steps are tool-aware. Per Q36 default, Connectors stay the abstraction — no new primitive class for 'external comm channels'; v1 EXTENDs existing Connectors (Slack live per memory project_connectors_slack_live; MCP servers; via plugin). The data model is Connector = {connector_id, tool_name, auth_config, inbound_handler, outbound_dispatcher}; a Workflow step declares connector_id to route via. Flow: inbound message → Connector handler emits artifact_registered (§3.2 #9) and the inbound Artifact joins the context catalog routable per 7a/B11; a step with connector_id fires outbound → Connector dispatches and emits a DecisionProvenance row per ADR-007 plus an outbound Artifact per B9. Failure handling stays canon: expired/revoked auth at dispatch routes via canon on_failure per §6.5; a Connector-emitted Artifact crossing a Tenant boundary fires HS-3 per §6.9.3. Telemetry events are artifact_registered, policy_decision, tenant_boundary_violation; metrics affected are Operator-Hours-Saved (Connectors directly displace manual cross-tool ferrying) and cross-company decision-replay success rate. The §4.11.A feature registry indexes B17 at blocks/B17-external-tools-connectors.md.sutra/os/native/blocks/B17-external-tools-connectors.md#1-line-summary

Shipped production — connector-token storage vs keys-in-env-vars HARD contract (proto/004)shipped-productionPROTO-004 is ACTIVE, HARD, enforced at two layers: (1) plugin L0 hooks/keys-in-env-vars.sh, PreToolUse on Write|Edit|MultiEdit, scans INCOMING content for provider-shaped key regexes (sk-…, AKIA…, ghp_…, ghs_…, xoxb-…, ya29.…, AIza…, BEGIN PRIVATE KEY — sourced from OWASP/git-secrets), exits 2 BLOCK with 'Put secrets in .env / env vars only' and a JSONL audit row to .enforcement/routing-misses.log; (2) the Asawa holding layer dispatcher-pretool.sh Check 5 blocks assignment-shaped (api_key|secret_key|password|token)[:=] content — the two pattern sets catch disjoint cases. Exemptions: env-type files (*.env, */secrets/*, *credentials*), privacy-scrub libs, binaries; only edits that INTRODUCE a secret are blocked (remediation edits pass); override PROTO004_ACK=1 + reason is audit-logged; default-OFF per D32 at plugin layer. The dossier's redo recipe directs Native to reconcile with §5.1 connector oauth JSON files by whitelisting ~/.sutra-connectors/oauth/** chmod-600 paths as a named legitimate secret home, mirroring the hook's *keys.json/*credentials* exemption.holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#proto/004 (Shipped today · Behavior contract · Redo recipe)
HS-9 hardstop — two-pattern secret boundary reconciling §5.1 oauth token storagenative-canonHS-9 canon (DRAFT v1, authored 2026-06-12; production contract ACTIVE + HARD in Sutra, Native runtime wiring pending): secrets live only in environment variables or env-type files; any artifact write introducing a key-shaped string into a non-env file is denied at the boundary BEFORE the file is touched (exit-2 class block); remediation edits that remove secrets always pass. The scope boundary reconciles the §5.1 connector-token tension: HS-9's enforcement boundary is the repository write path; out-of-repo secret stores are NOT silently exempt — they are a second, separately-governed sanctioned pattern: untracked stores outside any repository, mode 0600, under a named location (~/.sutra-connectors/oauth/ precedent), with edits gated by the adjacent secret-file edit gate (ADR-003). Two patterns, two gates — a secret in neither pattern violates one of them (deepseek MODIFY absorbed 2026-06-12). Native kill-switch key per B10 K4 registry mandate: SUTRA_HS9_KEYS_DISABLED. STRIDE relevance: Information Disclosure primary (credential exfiltration via repository distribution), Elevation of Privilege secondary.sutra/os/native/hardstops/HS-9-keys-in-env-vars.md#decision-fail-mode

ADR-005 — host enum lock and the connector-authentication implicationadr-decisionADR-005 (Accepted 2026-05-07) requires WorkflowStep.host ∈ {claude, codex} whenever action='invoke_host_llm'. host='claude' invokes claude --bare — recursion-safe; no plugin sync, no CLAUDE.md auto-load, no hooks; it skips keychain reads, so it is unauthenticated for connector calls. host='codex' invokes codex exec — defaults to a read-only sandbox, useful for analysis/review steps that should not mutate state. Per-step timeout_ms flows into host activity args; host-class default is 60s for claude --bare, ≥300s for codex. The tradeoff is locked to these two hosts in v1.x; the LLMSubstrate adapter for OpenAI/Gemini swap is deferred to backlog OS-21, and OS-20 (per-invocation MCP context passing for claude --bare) is also deferred. A stated consequence: steps that need BOTH authenticated mutation AND sandboxed analysis must split into two steps.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-005-host-llm-host-selection.md#context

§4 runtime integration — outbound[] envelope fragment + DEGRADED half-fail statemaster-specThe §4.3 state envelope carries a B4-produced fragment: outbound: [ { audience, channel, draft, sent_status, inbound_responses } ] — emitted 'if comms triggered'. OS process facility — single daemon process per operatormaster-specMaster §4.0 process model: 'Single background daemon process per operator. holding/website/native/_block-compute.html#L159-L163 (Internal parts: process facility row)
Cron / scheduling facility — daemon-side tick, not OS cron (ADR-017)adr-decisionADR-017 decides: 'Native engine MUST implement cadence scheduling as a daemon-side setInterval tick; MUST NOT depend on external cron / launchd.' CadenceScheduler.start(intervalMs) runs inside the daemon process; on each tick, scheduled cadences whose next-fire time falls within the ±5 min window (I-12) fire TriggerEvent rows, and each fire emits an EngineEvent + DecisionProvenance — replayable like any other Trigger. External cron/launchd was rejected as not portable across macOS/Linux, with no integration with Native's TriggerEvent lifecycle and no DecisionProvenance on fire; a cron-style escape hatch survives only as TriggerSpec.cadence.cron, evaluated by the daemon at tick time. Stated consequences: portable, auditable, ±5min verifiable inside the engine; but 'daemon must stay running for cadences to fire (start/stop discipline matters)' and per-tick CPU cost is non-zero. Master §4.0 cadence row: per-Workflow trigger spec with cadence field; the daemon fires triggers on schedule and dispatches matching Workflows; the operator 'sees the SCHEDULED OUTPUT (daily pulse, weekly synthesis, etc.); not the cron firing'. The second-order draft draws cron feeding the process facility twice: as a START trigger (alongside plugin launch and cold boot) and as recurring cadence ticks the daemon turns into Workflow dispatches per ADR-017.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-017-cron-daemon-cadence-tick.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-017-cron-daemon-cadence-tick.md#consequences
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-0-daemon (Cadence row)
holding/website/native/_block-compute.html#L164-L168 (Internal parts: cron row)
holding/website/native/_block-compute.html#L177 (Internal flow)
On-disk durability — fsync'd JSONL append (ADR-013)adr-decisionADR-013 decides Native MUST persist EngineEvent and DecisionProvenance as fsync'd JSONL append-only files on disk: one JSONL line per event/decision, UTF-8, fsync per append — 'never lose a row on crash'. A stderr beacon fallback fires when the primary path is unwritable, with dual fallback to /tmp (the HS-4 chain). The shape was chosen against four constraints — survives daemon restart, zero new dependency, append-only audit, multi-process safe (POSIX atomic line-level appends); in-memory ring buffer and SQLite were both rejected. Stated costs: disk I/O per event (fsync is not free), and no indexed query — replay is sequential scan. This is the durability contract for everything Compute stores under ~/.sutra-native/.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-013-telemetry-sink-fsync-jsonl.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-013-telemetry-sink-fsync-jsonl.md#context
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-013-telemetry-sink-fsync-jsonl.md#consequences
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-0-daemon (State persistence row)
Isolated replica kits via SUTRA_NATIVE_HOME (ADR-016)adr-decisionADR-016 decides: 'Native engine MUST honor SUTRA_NATIVE_HOME env var to redirect user-kit reads/writes; the Asawa replica MUST live at $HOME/.sutra-native-asawa-replica/.' Primary user-kit stays at $HOME/.sutra-native/user-kit/ (production / fleet / emergent); the replica gets distinct PID locks + distinct telemetry JSONL so 'daemons can run side-by-side without interference'. Adding the replica primitives to the primary kit was rejected for contamination risk: Workflow ID collision, mixed telemetry in the same JSONL, and no clean reset path. A reset script (bash holding/scripts/build-asawa-native-replica.sh) is idempotent — wipes + rebuilds the replica without touching primary. Stated costs: two daemons mean two memory + CPU footprints when both run, and the operator must remember to set SUTRA_NATIVE_HOME; the decision 'sets pattern for future per-tenant kit isolation as multi-tenant matures'.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-016-replica-as-isolated-user-kit.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-016-replica-as-isolated-user-kit.md#context
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-016-replica-as-isolated-user-kit.md#consequences
Cloud-hosted compute — deferred v2+, activation-gatedsecond-order-draftThe second-order draft's internal-parts table defines cloud-hosted compute as a 'managed-infra distribution channel where the operator does not run their own daemon', deferred behind activation gates: 'multi-tenant infra spec + HS-3 tenant-boundary extension to shared-host, per-tenant data-residency + key-isolation, pricing tier, cloud-managed install path. Until then, Compute = operator-local machine only.' Master §1.4.5 carries the canonical activation row: (1) multi-tenant infra spec authored + HS-3 tenant-boundary contract extends to shared-host; (2) per-tenant data-residency + key-isolation guarantees; (3) pricing tier defined (operator-economics validated per §C.4 R8); (4) onboarding flow (§6.3) gets a cloud-managed install path matrix row. The draft's internal-flow text adds: 'The deferred cloud-hosted variant (v2+) would replace the operator-local machine entirely, with no schema impact described yet.'holding/website/native/_block-compute.html#L169-L173 (Internal parts: cloud row)

No hardware/OS requirements for Compute anywhere in the doc (supported OS, CPU/memory/disk minimums) — what does an operator's machine need? (_block-compute.html gap 1)

Compute failure semantics beyond 'cold boot → START': what happens to in-flight Workflow executions when the machine sleeps, crashes, or loses power mid-run? (_block-compute.html gap 3)

No resource governance on the operator's machine: nothing caps daemon CPU/disk usage or defines retention/rotation for decision-provenance.jsonl and native.log growth. (_block-compute.html gap 5)
Authority + Tenancy 'gates every access' wraps the Compute box in the diagram, but no compute-level enforcement mechanism (disk encryption, key storage location, local access control) is named. (_block-compute.html gap 6)
Section 1.0 gives Compute no pillar-differentiated role — does B5 'keep it mine' imply local-compute data residency on the host machine, or is Compute a uniform substrate for all pillars? (_matrix.html open question)

§4 Native Platform — workforce-deployment substrate (ownership frame)master-spec§4's 6-block frame: Purpose = 'The substrate that scopes, deploys, and supervises a workforce of agents. CoS (and future products) ride on it.' Owns = 'Charters (fixed/flex) · Agents (on-demand materialization) · Method routing per task tier · Authority gates · State envelope between pillars · Audit substrate (DecisionProvenance).' Depends on §5 Connector layer for any out-of-process tool call and §7 Observability for platform health; does NOT own operator surface, pillar UX, pricing, channels. The callout: 'Native is the engine room. Operators don't see Native directly — they see CoS (§2).' Detailed primitive definitions live in §9 D.10 (Charter Model) and D.11 (Method Selection); §4 surfaces runtime behavior + state.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-native-platform (6-block table + callout)
§4.0 Runtime daemon — where Native lives + runsmaster-specNative runs as a per-operator local daemon; the operator doesn't see it (§A.2.2 post-onboarding zero-terminal); it 'manages workforce deployment, audit-log writes, hook firing, and cadence scheduling silently in the background'. Home directory: ~/.sutra-native/ per-operator daemon root containing user-kit (charters, domains, triggers, workflows), decision-provenance.jsonl, native.log. Process model: single background daemon process per operator, started by the Sutra plugin at session boot OR explicit 'sutra native start', re-exec across plugin updates without state loss. Lifecycle: START (plugin launch / cron / cold boot) → RUNNING (handles workflow dispatches + hook firings + cadence ticks + audit writes) → STOP → RESTART (state restored from user-kit + decision-provenance log), operator-visible only via the Daily Pulse health row (§7). State persistence is 3-channel audit (DecisionProvenance JSONL + stderr beacon + tmp fallback per §4.10 HS-4 + ADR-013) plus user-kit markdown files per ADR-008. Boundary: per-Tenant isolated (HS-3 + ADR-006); cross-tenant calls require an explicit delegates_to edge; default-deny on cross-tenant data flow. Canon link: full daemon runtime ships at sutra/marketplace/native/bin/sutra-native + sutra/marketplace/native/src/ — path corrected 2026-05-13 per §10.10 P1 #5 (shipped runtime lives under sutra/marketplace/native/, not sutra/marketplace/plugin/).holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-0-daemon
§4.1 — 10 canon primitivesnative-canonNative runtime uses 10 typed primitives per canon at sutra/os/native/primitives/: Domain, Charter, Workflow, Step, Trigger, ExecutionResult, EngineEvent, Tenant, Approval, DecisionProvenance — 'PRD body describes the operator-facing capability of each; engine type signatures + invariants + lifecycle live in canon.' What's NOT a primitive (older PRD had these): 'Agent' (agent behavior is composed from Workflow + Step + Charter scope + Authority gate), 'Method routing' (per-Step config + HOW Engine in §3), 'Audit substrate' (replaced by DecisionProvenance primitive + 3-channel ADR-013). Canon adds the engine-level facts: Workflow id is content-addressed W-hash (sha256 of canonical form — any field change yields a new Workflow id, immutable, new mint required); Step is skill_ref XOR action (I-3, mint-time HARD reject); ExecutionResult state is the 6-set {running, success, failed, awaiting_approval, paused, declared_gap} with failure_reason null IFF state ∈ {success, declared_gap} (I-4).holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-1-primitives

Execution lifecycle + terminal-event uniqueness (I-14)native-canonCanon: 'every Execution has EXACTLY ONE terminal event from the set {workflow_completed, workflow_failed, approval_requested}'; rollback events (workflow_rollback_started → _complete | _partial) are RECOVERY transitions occurring AFTER workflow_failed under failure_policy='rollback' — they are NOT themselves terminal. Workflow lifecycle: Mint (LiteExecutor validates schema + invariants; content-addressed id; row persisted to user-kit registry) → Dormant → Triggered (TriggerSpec match or direct run CLI) → Running (preconditions cleared, workflow_started emits, step[0] dispatches) → step transitions → Terminal → Recovery (if rollback) → Decommission (registry entry deprecated=true prevents new Executions; existing Executions reach terminal independently; no field overwrite). ExecutionResult adds: when state === 'awaiting_approval', a persisted ledger row at user-kit/pending-approvals/E-<id>.json MUST exist (I-15); logs[] is append-only — never edit, never delete past events; partial=true ONLY when failure_policy='continue' advanced past a failed Step.sutra/os/native/primitives/workflow.md#lifecycle-created--terminal-states

EngineEvent — append-only 26-event typed audit catalognative-canonEngineEvent is 'Native's append-only typed audit row — one JSONL line per event', carrying event_type, ts_ms, execution_id?, payload, and agent_identity (per ADR-015); the catalog of 26 event_type values is exhaustive — new event-types require a NATIVE-ENGINE.md amendment + ADR, mint-time reject on unknown type. Invariants: monotonic ts_ms (replay safety — events must not be re-ordered after persistence); append-only immutability (replay derives full state from the log); per-event payload schema validation; I-16 commitment_broken referential integrity (must reference a resolvable Charter obligation id); I-9 + I-17 policy_decision must carry policy_id AND policy_version; execution_id may be null ONLY for events outside an Execution lifecycle (e.g. routing_decision pre-match). Sinks: ~/.sutra-native/user-kit/executions/E-<hash>.events.jsonl (per-Execution), user-kit/events.jsonl (global replay source), user-kit/decision-provenance.jsonl (DecisionProvenance subset), fsync-on-write per ADR-013. Master §4.11 deliberately does not duplicate the event names: 'readers should consult canon for the exhaustive list'.sutra/os/native/primitives/engine-event.md#purpose
sutra/os/native/primitives/engine-event.md#invariants-must-hold

6 runtime surfaces — ROUTE · RUN · GATE · EMERGE · AUDIT · TENANT (RUN contract detail)native-canonPer §4.11, the 6 surfaces are 'runtime interfaces between daemon + workflows + operator': ROUTE (operator utterance → Workflow selection), RUN (workflow execution — step machine + retry semantics), GATE (Authority + Approval), EMERGE (pattern propose/approve, ADR-010), AUDIT (DecisionProvenance + EngineEvent durable sink, ADR-013), TENANT (cross-tenant boundary, default-deny per ADR-006 + HS-3). RUN canon: fire a matched Workflow → dispatch each WorkflowStep → for action='invoke_host_llm' steps, hand the prompt to the configured host (claude --bare or codex exec) → persist a typed Execution log; reached via NativeEngine.run(workflowId, ctx) from handleHSutraEvent or via CLI 'sutra-native run <workflow-id>'. RUN invariants include: exactly one workflow_started per fired Workflow (RUN-I1); exactly one step_started per step dispatch (RUN-I2); per-step timeout_ms defaults to host-class default when null — 60s for claude --bare, ≥300s expected for complex codex tasks (RUN-I7). 'RUN executes within the Native daemon. The host-LLM session is the effector boundary (§5.1) — file/git/network mutations declared in the Workflow happen there, not in Native. Native persists EngineEvents; the host writes the actual side effects.' Declared canon gap: NativeEngine.on_host_llm_result resumption wire is open per §8 OS-1 — 'RUN's contract for awaiting that callback is implementation-shaped, not canon-shaped'.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-11-runtime-catalog (Surfaces row + §4.11.A surfaces table)

§4.2 workforce state machine + §4.10.2 failure-state extensionsmaster-specHappy path: PROPOSED (operator adds Charter) → operator approves → ACTIVE (Charter live) → new task (T0/T1/T2/T3) → agent materializes (scoped to Charter) → task completes → PERSISTED (artifact + audit row, output → B5.a/b/c/d) → operator revokes / scope-expires / supersedes → RETIRED (Charter inactive · audit replayable forever). §4.10.2 extends it with three failure states: PAUSED — entered when a hardstop fires or Charter Authority requires explicit operator decision; operator sees 'task paused at step X — decision needed'; exit via operator decision → ACTIVE (resume) or RETIRED (cancel). ESCALATED — entered when the B3 retry budget is exhausted (per §4.5) or the Authority gate triggers the ESCALATE class (ADR-018 contract 1); operator sees 'auto-retry exhausted; your judgment needed' with B3a THINK 3-angle brainstorm; exit via operator confirming direction → ACTIVE (B3b auto-executes) or RETIRED. DEGRADED — entered on connector half-failure (send succeeded but audit write missed) or partial side-effect; operator sees the partial-completion message with compensation options; exit via reconciliation step (idempotent retry or manual log-write) → ACTIVE or PAUSED.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-2-state-machine
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-10-2-failure-states
§4.3 state envelope + §4.4 acceptance criteriamaster-specPillar-to-pillar handoffs carry a typed envelope; CoS's 5 pillars consume + produce envelope fragments, and 'Native enforces shape + authority'. The envelope fields: turn_id, operator_id, charter_ref (which Charter scopes this turn), task_tier (T0/T1/T2/T3), intent {type, sub_intents} from B1, context_envelope {my_own, worlds_best, peer_sutras_v2} from B2, decision_trail [{phase, factors, authority_verdict, output_ref}] from B3, outbound [{audience, channel, draft, sent_status, inbound_responses}] from B4, b5_writes {active_session_ref, provenance_row_ref, learned_pattern_updates} to B5. §4.4 acceptance criteria (Native platform v1): Charter create/update/retire is operator-only and agent materialization is Native-internal; Authority gate fires at every external-effect call (send · write · delete · pay); the state envelope round-trips with zero data loss across pillar handoffs (HS-4 fires if it doesn't); audit substrate writes 100% of decisions to triple-channel (gate-log + provenance + artifact); cross-tenant queries default-deny.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-3-state-envelope
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-4-acceptance
§4.5 test-before-conclude gate (accuracy primitive)master-spec'Native does NOT mark a task done before testing the output. Accuracy is a per-workflow KPI. Test happens BEFORE concluding, not after. Output is rejected back to B3 retry if test fails.' Five steps: (1) pre-declare test per tier — T0 grep / file-exists / contract-match; T1 step-by-step verify + final integration check; T2 operator-visible 1-min iteration verification; T3 milestone-gate verification at goal-transition points; (2) B3 produces output (auto-execute path); (3) run the pre-declared test — 'Determinism: same input + same Charter → same expected output shape'; (4) conditional conclude — pass → conclude + write audit row + present to operator; fail → loop to B3 retry (max N retries per Charter), after N failures escalate to operator (Authority gate); (5) post-conclude — operator feedback (drop / accept / refine) becomes test signal for B5.d learned patterns, thresholds adapt per workflow. Rationale: testing before-conclude means 'CoS catches its own failures and retries silently'; the §7 accuracy KPI measures % of outputs passing first-time-test vs needing retry.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-5-test-before-conclude
ADR-004 registry-and-effector split + ADR-005 host selectionadr-decisionADR-004: 'Native engine MUST be the registry + audit layer; the founder's interactive host-LLM session MUST be the effector that performs declared mutations.' Native owns Workflow JSON, Trigger predicates, Execution rows, EngineEvent audit, DecisionProvenance, Tenant isolation; Claude (founder session) owns filesystem writes, git operations, network calls; the split is recorded on every Execution via the agent_identity chain (ADR-015). Consequences: daemon stays unauthenticated and recursion-safe (no credential exfil surface); audit records intent BEFORE the effector runs; autonomous 'fire-and-forget' workflows are blocked at v1.0 (deferred to v1.3 per OS-1/OS-2). ADR-005: 'Native engine MUST require WorkflowStep.host ∈ {claude, codex} whenever action='invoke_host_llm''. host='claude' invokes claude --bare (recursion-safe — recursive Claude without --bare re-loads the same governance pipeline, doubling token cost and potentially recursing forever); host='codex' invokes codex exec (read-only sandbox, default for analysis/review/planning). Per-step timeout_ms flows into host activity args; host-class defaults 60s for claude --bare, ≥300s for codex; the two-host set is locked in v1.x with an LLMSubstrate adapter deferred (OS-21). Steps needing BOTH authenticated mutation AND sandboxed analysis must split into two steps.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-004-registry-and-effector-split.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-004-registry-and-effector-split.md#consequences
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-005-host-llm-host-selection.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-005-host-llm-host-selection.md#context
ADR-009 approval gate — typed pause + utterance-driven resumeadr-decisionADR-009: 'Native engine MUST add requires_approval: bool at both Workflow and WorkflowStep levels, and emit/handle the typed event triplet approval_requested → approval_granted | approval_denied.' A step with requires_approval=true causes the executor to emit approval_requested, the Execution to transition to awaiting_approval, and a ledger row to persist at user-kit/pending-approvals/E-<id>.json (I-15) — the row survives daemon restart so pending approvals are recoverable. Founder utterance 'approve E-<id>' parses to NativeEngine.resumeApproved(execId) and continues at step_index+1; 'reject E-<id> <reason>' yields approval_denied and terminal workflow_failed. Re-fire on a resolved approval emits approval_already_handled — idempotent, keeping I-14's exactly-one-terminal contract. An inline Y/N stdin gate was rejected as physically impossible (daemon has no controlling TTY); an external approval-queue subsystem was rejected because splitting state across two registries makes replay ambiguous. Multi-party / quorum approval is deferred (OS-15).sutra/os/decisions/ADR-009-approval-gate-primitive.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-009-approval-gate-primitive.md#context
sutra/os/native/primitives/step.md#lifecycle-created--terminal-states
sutra/os/native/primitives/execution-result.md#invariants-must-hold (I-15)
ADR-011 on_failure policy — closed 5-setadr-decisionADR-011: 'Native engine MUST define step.on_failure as exactly one of {rollback, escalate, pause, abort, continue} — closed 5-set, no extension without ADR.' Effects per policy: rollback compensates steps in reverse and emits workflow_rollback_started → _complete or _partial; escalate emits workflow_escalated and routes to the founder channel; pause emits step_paused, persists a queue entry, resumes on signal; abort emits workflow_failed immediately; continue logs the failure, advances to step[i+1], sets partial=true, and skips outputs validation for the failed step. Workflow.failure_policy is the default; step.on_failure overrides per-step; each policy has a typed event family (events 4, 7, 19-24) — no free-form failure log. A boolean retry was rejected as too coarse; a custom callback per Workflow was rejected as non-deterministic and non-replayable. A sixth policy requires a new ADR + new event types + parser + executor branch — 'extension cost is deliberate'.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-011-on-failure-policy-five-set.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-011-on-failure-policy-five-set.md#context
sutra/os/native/primitives/step.md#type-signature-typescript-style
ADR-012 PNC predicates — typed parser, not trusted proseadr-decisionADR-012: 'Native engine MUST parse Workflow.preconditions, Workflow.postconditions, Workflow.failure_policy, and step.on_failure as typed machine-checkable predicates — not free prose.' Parser surface: match-all / match-any / cron / typed comparison against execution context (Tenant, Domain, Charter obligation id, prior EngineEvent). Evaluated at runtime via precondition_check (event 10) and postcondition_check (event 11); commitment_broken (event 25) references a Charter obligation id resolvable in the registry (I-16); F-10 forbids English-only routing/gating positions at terminal_check (HARD reject at primitive-mint). Evidence behind the decision: the 13-case run report showed prose PNC 'declared at primitive-mint but never eval-checked at runtime — silent guarantee failure'; LLM-eval of prose at runtime was rejected as non-deterministic, non-replayable, and an injection surface. A hybrid is allowed for documentation only: prose annotation is advisory per ADR-008, the typed core is load-bearing.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-012-pnc-typed-parser-over-prose.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-012-pnc-typed-parser-over-prose.md#context
sutra/os/native/primitives/trigger.md#invariants-must-hold
ADR-007 DecisionProvenance schema + ADR-013 fsync'd JSONL sinkadr-decisionADR-007: 'Native engine MUST emit a typed DecisionProvenance row for every consequential decision (I-7), with policy_id AND policy_version non-empty (I-9, I-17).' Schema: id (uuid v4), ts_ms, agent_identity (chain), policy_id, policy_version, scope ∈ {WORKFLOW, STEP, HOOK, TENANT, CUTOVER}, outcome ∈ {allow, deny, pause, escalate}, reason (sanitized: no colons / no newlines), data_refs[] each carrying authoritative_status per ADR-008. PolicyDispatcher.evaluate(scope, evidence) returns the row; emitters append via UserKit.appendDecisionProvenance with fsync (ADR-013); DecisionProvenance is the replay surface — reconstructible by re-running the policy dispatcher on recorded evidence, and policy-version drift is detectable. ADR-013: EngineEvent and DecisionProvenance persist as fsync'd JSONL append-only files — one line per event, fsync per append, stderr beacon fallback when the primary path is unwritable, dual fallback to /tmp (the HS-4 chain); in-memory ring buffer and SQLite were rejected against the four constraints (survives restart, zero dependency, append-only audit, multi-process safe). The pattern detector (ADR-010) reads the log directly — no in-memory cache, restart-safe.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-007-decision-provenance-schema.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-013-telemetry-sink-fsync-jsonl.md#decision

ADR-006 tenant isolation via Domain.tenant_idadr-decisionADR-006: 'Native engine MUST model tenant isolation as a first-class required field Domain.tenant_id (T-hash) referencing a Tenant primitive.' Every Domain belongs to exactly one Tenant (I-13, required field, no nullable); Execution.tenant_context carries {tenant_id} and TenantIsolation.assertCrossTenantAllowed(src, dst, op) is called unconditionally when a step touches a different tenant; cross-tenant ops without an explicit delegates_to edge fail closed and emit tenant_boundary_violation (HS-3, I-8). Cohort was rejected as the carrier because it is a routing/targeting primitive (a Workflow can target multiple Cohorts; a Domain belongs to exactly one Tenant); the D33 settings.json firewall stays as defense-in-depth at the Claude Code config layer — Native does NOT depend on it. The tenant boundary is a typed schema field checked at primitive-mint AND execution, and the audit trail records every cross-tenant attempt. Hierarchical/nested tenancy is declared in schema but execution semantics are v1.x backlog.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-006-tenant-isolation-domain-field.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-006-tenant-isolation-domain-field.md#consequences

ADR-010 organic emergence — propose-approve at k≥4 (EMERGE surface)adr-decisionADR-010: 'Native engine MUST require explicit founder approval via approve P-<id> utterance to register any pattern-detected Workflow; the detector MUST propose only at k≥4 utterance matches.' The pattern detector reads the H-Sutra JSONL (ADR-013) and tracks per-pattern hit count + sample utterances; at k≥4 with no registered workflow matching, it emits pattern_proposed (event 12) with draft Workflow JSON + sample utterances; 'approve P-<id>' → proposal_approved (event 13) → register Workflow; 'reject P-<id> <reason>' → proposal_rejected (event 14). Auto-register was rejected (no founder oversight, no naming, no PNC review — 'can mint workflows from typos'); k=4 was set because 'k=2 is too noisy, k=3 still ambiguous, k=4 is the smallest k where a pattern is consistently a real workflow'. Sample utterances ship with the proposal so the founder can audit the matched evidence; smarter LLM-drafted Workflows on first match are deferred to v1.x backlog.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-010-organic-emergence-propose-approve.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-010-organic-emergence-propose-approve.md#context
TriggerSpec primitive + ADR-017 cadence schedulingnative-canonTriggerSpec declares WHEN a Workflow fires: pattern ∈ {preprocessor, observer, gate, fan_out, negotiation} (closed 5-set), a typed predicate (match-all / match-any / cron — never free prose, HARD rejected at mint per ADR-012), target_workflow_id that MUST resolve in the user-kit registry (mint-time HARD reject if unresolved; non-firing if target later deprecated), and an optional cadence object per ADR-017. The Router consults Triggers for every HSutraEvent; on match it emits routing_decision (event 1) and dispatches NativeEngine.run(target_workflow_id, ctx); on miss it emits routing_decision with no matched_workflow_id or routes to the ADR-010 pattern-proposal flow when k≥4 unmatched utterances accumulate. Cadence-driven triggers fire via CadenceScheduler.tick() within ±5 min of schedule (I-12), each fire emitting EngineEvent + DecisionProvenance per ADR-017's daemon-side setInterval decision. Trigger terminal state is NOT specified in canon §2.5 — triggers are effectively permanent once minted in v1.0; a deprecated flag is a future-ADR implementation choice.sutra/os/native/primitives/trigger.md#type-signature-typescript-style
sutra/os/native/primitives/trigger.md#invariants-must-hold
sutra/os/native/primitives/trigger.md#lifecycle-created--terminal-states

ADR-008 authoritative-vs-advisory status on every DataRefadr-decisionADR-008: 'Native engine MUST require every DataRef to carry an explicit authoritative_status ∈ {authoritative, advisory} field.' Readers honor authoritative over advisory at lookup time — declarative precedence, no convention; Workflow inputs[] and outputs[] tag each entry, enforced at primitive-mint via ajv schema validation; DecisionProvenance.data_refs[] carries the same field so every audit row records which version of which surface was load-bearing. The decision resolves the markdown-vs-code conflict: 'code always wins' breaks when markdown captures a NEW direction not yet wired; 'markdown wins' breaks when code is the live runtime contract; collapsing to one surface was rejected because the founder reads markdown and the daemon reads code. Drift detection runs as a weekly cadence reporting the authoritative-vs-advisory diff per artifact pair. Cost: every DataRef author must set the field — no implicit default, ajv rejects on omission.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-008-markdown-vs-code-authoritative-state.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-008-markdown-vs-code-authoritative-state.md#context

ADR-014 depth router as Native's own Workflow (W-load-native-context)adr-decisionADR-014: 'Native engine MUST implement the depth-router as the W-load-native-context Workflow — fires codex exec, emits a structured load-plan JSON via return_contract, persists an Execution row.' The Workflow uses host='codex' (read-only sandbox per ADR-005) — analysis only, no mutation; inputs are turn context, depth signal, task class slug; outputs are a load-plan JSON {include: [paths], exclude: [paths], rationale} schema-validated by return_contract. Every depth-router fire is replayable from its Execution row; the pattern is 'the canonical example of Native runs Native', and reuse_tag=true makes it discoverable as a Skill via SkillEngine.resolve. Bash and inline-LLM alternatives were rejected for lacking audit rows, return_contract validation, and replay. Costs: per-fire Workflow boot + codex subprocess (~300ms baseline); a first fast-path measurement may force a hybrid (bash fast-path + Workflow slow-path).sutra/os/decisions/ADR-014-depth-router-via-workflow.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-014-depth-router-via-workflow.md#consequences
ADR-022 full-loop six mechanisms (system-of-work science adoption)adr-decisionADR-022 (accepted 2026-06-12) adopts six dependency-ordered mechanisms: (1) SoR truth upgrade — bracket external effects with attempted/result events, persist LLM outputs AS events, deterministic replay, event schema versioning; (2) registration-at-birth — new workflow declares contract + reads/writes (→ where-used index) + version; (3) workflow authoring engine — observe situation → Cynefin classify → draft typed Workflow with per-step contracts → machine soundness check → ADR-010 propose/approve; (4) comparator + admission — reference limits per workflow + escalation, open-Execution WIP cap gates new runs; (5) the miner — scheduled read of the record proposing extraction/revision/retirement via propose/approve; (6) industry packs — templates + record schemas + reference limits per domain, 'engine stays domain-free'. The loop (design → execute → record → compare → redesign) closes at #5; sequence 1→2→3→4→5 with 6 parallel after 3. The honest-state finding: 'Native today runs design → execute → record. The record fails both litmus tests: not replayable (state cannot be rebuilt from the log alone) and not regulated (no decision rule reads it in-loop) — so the SoR is telemetry, not yet a system of record.' Mechanisms #3 and #5 each ship with their own ADR; charter Contract/Primitives edits land when #1/#2 ship.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-022-full-loop-six-mechanisms.md#decision
sutra/os/decisions/ADR-022-full-loop-six-mechanisms.md#context

§4.10.1 hardstop catalog HS-1..HS-8 + MECHANISM-PENDING hedgemaster-spec'Native's hardstop catalog (HS-1..HS-8) defines what the system does when it cannot continue safely'; the full engine spec lives at sutra/os/native/hardstops/HS-*.md and §4.10 is the operator-visible summary. Operator-visible behaviors: HS-1 system REFUSES to self-modify ('this change touches Sutra's own rules — please ratify directly'); HS-2 aborts the current turn when per-turn governance overhead exceeds 25% of token budget (canon HS-2 part-file: soft target ≤15% per I-6, HARD-STOP >25%, per-turn-scoped recovery — next turn proceeds normally); HS-3 cross-company action DENIED without explicit delegates_to; HS-4 system STOPS when DecisionProvenance is unwritable across all 3 fallback channels ('no silent unaudited execution'); HS-5 cutover PAUSED with operator + Tenant owner HITL; HS-6 SESSION SUSPENDED when 3+ distinct governance hooks fail in one turn; HS-7 shipment broadly BLOCKED when the codex review queue exceeds 20 items or any item is >7 days stale; HS-8 auto-ROLLBACK on production canary regression, founder notified. Hardstop UX contract: every hardstop produces a 1-2 sentence operator-facing message, a recommended NEXT ACTION, and a link to the audit row. The MECHANISM-PENDING callout (2026-05-13, §10.10 row 1): 'HS-4 · HS-5 · HS-6 · HS-7 · HS-8 are documented as canonical hardstops above, but the impl-vs-docs audit confirmed live detectors/executors do NOT yet exist in the shipped Native runtime — those rows are doctrine, mechanism pending. HS-1, HS-2, HS-3 have at least partial enforcement via existing plugin hooks.'holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-10-1-hardstops

HS-9 keys-in-env-vars — canon DRAFT lesson-port from production PROTO-004native-canonCanon hardstops/ contains a ninth part-file beyond master's HS-1..HS-8 list: HS-9 'Keys in env vars only' (DRAFT v1, authored 2026-06-12, 'lesson-port from ACTIVE production PROTO-004; not yet wired in Native runtime'). Fail-mode: 'Secrets live only in environment variables or env-type files. Any artifact write introducing a key-shaped string into a non-env file is denied at the boundary BEFORE the file is touched (exit-2 class block; the write never lands). Remediation edits that remove secrets always pass.' Two pattern families port verbatim because they catch disjoint cases: provider-shaped (sk-…, AKIA…, ghp_…, ghs_…, xoxb-…, ya29.…, AIza…, BEGIN PRIVATE KEY) and assignment-shaped (dispatcher Check-5). The two-pattern boundary reconciles §5.1 connector tokens: out-of-repo stores are a separately-governed sanctioned pattern — untracked, mode 0600, named location (~/.sutra-connectors/oauth/ precedent), edits gated by ADR-003 (deepseek MODIFY absorbed 2026-06-12); Native kill-switch key SUTRA_HS9_KEYS_DISABLED. Production dossier proto/004 confirms the contract is ACTIVE + HARD at two layers today (plugin keys-in-env-vars.sh PreToolUse exit-2 + holding dispatcher-pretool.sh Check 5), with an audited PROTO004_ACK override and the gap statement: 'Native names the hook in §4.11.A but states no key-handling invariant... Production has a precise, two-layer HARD contract... that Native has not captured as a hardstop' — now captured as this DRAFT part-file.sutra/os/native/hardstops/HS-9-keys-in-env-vars.md#decision-fail-mode

§4.8 Capability Surface Map (CSM) — feature lifecycle visible to operatorsmaster-specNative + CoS ship features through an explicit lifecycle; CSM is the source-of-truth for what's stable vs proposed, seen via the sutra-capability skill and SessionStart banner. States: cap-NNN PROPOSED (logged in CAPABILITY-MAP.md, not invocable) → CANDIDATE (under codex/deepseek review) → DESIGNED (architecture + acceptance ratified) → BUILT (merged, under §4.5 verification) → SHIPPING (verified, rolling out per §6.0 T1→T2→T3→T4 cadence) → LIVE/STABLE (default-on) → DEPRECATED (sunset per §6.4). The SHIPPING axis note (N38 resolution, 2026-06-12): 'this is the per-CAPABILITY rollout cadence — dogfood-first. It is a different axis from CLIENT-ONBOARDING order, which per D41 runs new-clients-first... Both hold; no contradiction.' Capability tiering across the fleet: L0 fleet-shipped (all T2-T4 via marketplace), L1 cohort-staged (with promotion deadline), L2 instance-local (T1 Asawa only — governance/experimental). Note: 'capabilities are atomic primitives; Modules (§1.4) are packaged compositions OF capabilities. CSM tracks both at different granularity.'holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-8-csm
§4.9 module deployment — 3-artifact contractmaster-specA Module deploys via 3 artifacts — 'a conceptual contract first; filesystem layout below is one canonical implementation': SPEC (module code + manifest + JSON schema for binding + dependencies on platform Capabilities; sutra/marketplace/plugin/modules/<name>/; shared single-source-of-truth), BINDING (per-Deployment config: Charter scope, audience, authority thresholds, operator-set parameters; ~/.sutra/instances/<operator>/<company>/modules/<name>.json; per-Deployment overlay), RUNTIME STATE (executions, snapshots, logs, caches, audit rows, learned patterns; holding/state/modules/<name>/<tenant-id>/; per-Deployment isolated). Deployment flow: operator installs → BINDING created for this operator + Charter (Authority gate fires on first run) → Module runs → RUNTIME STATE accumulates in a per-tenant isolated subtree, audit rows → §2.B B5.c, metrics → §7. Acceptance criteria: SPEC update is operator-invisible (rolls in via plugin update per §6.4); BINDING is operator-editable within §1.4.3 deployment authority phases and Charter-scoped; RUNTIME STATE per-tenant isolated with cross-tenant access default-denied (the §4.9.2 row says 'HS-2 fires on violation'); retire/decommission pins SPEC, archives BINDING, preserves RUNTIME STATE for a replay window then trims per D.12 Artifact Governance.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-9-module-deployment
Production dossier proto/002 — fan-out barrier/join rule (lesson for the Execution engine)shipped-productionProduction retired PROTO-002 as a protocol ('agent-execution concern, not a system invariant') but ships the lesson fleet-wide as plugin hook agent-completion-check.sh (PostToolUse on Bash|Edit|Write, SOFT exit-0): when heuristic signals of still-running parallel agents exist, the orchestrator gets the verbatim rule 'Do NOT write synthesis/output until ALL agents complete. Read ALL agent outputs before proceeding. Do NOT substitute your own work for pending agent work.' Redo recipe for Native: 'Native should NOT port the /tmp-mtime heuristic — it is a workaround for not owning the execution substrate. Native's daemon owns Execution rows with terminal states, so reimplement as a first-class barrier invariant: an orchestrating Execution may not transition to synthesis/terminal while any sibling_group member Execution is non-terminal; on violation, block or queue rather than warn.' The dossier ports the doctrine sentence and two origin incidents (Maze HOD 2026-04-04 orchestrator wrote report over 3 running agents and missed 6 bugs; Dharmik SEO 2026-04-07 compiled before 4 agents returned) as rationale. Gap vs Native: 'Native has sibling_group lineage and B13 artifact locks but no stated barrier/join rule.'holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#proto-002

Production dossier hook/check-graduation — SOFT→HARD promotion pipeline constantsshipped-productionProduction ships a 3-script graduation framework: compliance-tracker.sh appends {ts,hook,status} JSONL rows per SOFT-hook pass/warn; check-graduation.sh computes per-hook pass% and prints GRADUATE (promote to HARD) when total ≥20 events AND pass <80%, KEEP SOFT at ≥80%, WAITING under 20 events — report-only, never flips enforcement itself; resilience.sh tracks consecutive fails, persisting state + warning at 2 consecutive and auto-escalating to a founder-visible os/feedback-to-sutra/ file at ≥3. Redo recipe: 'Port the constants verbatim: 20-event observation window, 80% compliance threshold, 2-consecutive persist / 3-consecutive escalate. Redesign the plumbing as Native primitives: every SOFT-policy evaluation emits a pass/warn EngineEvent into a compliance stream...; a graduation evaluator (workflow or scheduled job) computes per-policy verdicts...; resilience escalation becomes an event-threshold rule.' Promotion stays founder-gated — the only real flip that ever happened (2026-05-10 marker hooks) was founder-directed manual, so 'the framework's role is evidence-for-decision, not auto-promotion'. Gap vs Native: 'Native defines SOFT/HARD/CONVENTION tiers but has no promotion pipeline between them: no compliance event stream, no numeric graduation thresholds or observation window, no GRADUATE/KEEP/WAITING report, and no consecutive-failure escalation path.'holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#hookcheck-graduation
Production dossier proto/023 — per-instance runtime config surface (kill-switch registry)shipped-productionProduction ships one shell-sourceable file per instance at $HOME/.sutra/config.env holding every kill-switch, threshold, and opt-in, plus a bin/sutra-config CLI (get / set / list / init / path; init idempotent; set preserves inline comments). Default config ships kill-switches (SUTRA_RTK_ENABLED, SUTRA_CODEX_DIRECTIVE_ENABLED, SUTRA_ESTIMATION_COLLECTOR_ENABLED, SUTRA_COMPLETION_PROTOCOL_ENABLED), thresholds (SUTRA_CODEX_TIMEOUT_MS=600000, SUTRA_DEPTH_DEFAULT=5, SUTRA_BUILD_LAYER_DEFAULT=L0), observability (SUTRA_TELEMETRY=off), and tier (SUTRA_TIER=governance); config is ADDITIVE — existing env-var overrides keep working. §4.6 HOW bridge + §4.7 workforce outputs as deliverablesmaster-spec§4.6: every Native primitive embeds a HOW choice from §3 — Charters declare HOW preferences at creation, agents materialize with HOW context inherited, method routing (D.11) implements HOW-tier mapping, the authority gate (D.6) defers to HOW's escalation logic; 'Native runtime is the executor; §3 HOW is the doctrine.' §4.7: the workforce produces software + agentic systems with three delivery destinations — D1 shipped to client (client owns; Native deletes its copy post-handoff), D2 shipped back to Sutra-org (ratified into plugin/platform per §6 lifecycle), D3 operator-internal use (operator keeps in their Native workspace). Failure states (master §4.10.2)

StateEntry conditionOperator visibilityExit path
PAUSEDHardstop fires; OR Charter Authority requires explicit operator decision (D.6)'task paused at step X — decision needed' + recommended next actionOperator decides → ACTIVE (resume) OR RETIRED (cancel)

on_failure closed 5-set (ADR-011)

PolicyEffect
rollbackCompensate steps in reverse; emit workflow_rollback_started → _complete or _partial
escalateEmit workflow_escalated; route to founder channel
pauseEmit step_paused; persist queue entry; resume on signal
abortEmit workflow_failed immediately
continueLog failure; advance to step[i+1]; set partial=true; skip outputs validation for failed step; do NOT abort

§8 OS-1: NativeEngine.on_host_llm_result resumption wire is open — RUN's contract for awaiting the host-LLM callback is implementation-shaped, not canon-shaped (surfaces/run.md canon gap); autonomous fire-and-forget workflows blocked at v1.0, deferred to v1.3 per OS-1/OS-2 (ADR-004 consequences).
OS-15: multi-party / quorum approval shape deferred until first use case lands (ADR-009).
