▸ how — engineering detail

MechanismKindWhat exists (compiled — zero invention)Sources
§3 section contract — purpose, ownership, boundarymaster-spec§3 is "Native's IP: not 'do the work' — 'do the work via the right way of looking at this task.'" It owns the Lens primitive, the axes (Realization + Reflection as the recommended pair; many others available), the value-is-an-axis recursion, the examples, the decision procedure, and the operator-facing surface of HOW. It depends on §2 task tier (T0-T3), §4 Native runtime (where Lens choices manifest), and §7 Observability (where Lens accuracy is measured). It does NOT own execution (§4 runtime), operator surface (§2), transport (§5), or "the DAG / build / Native-primitive implementation (Claude-suggested, archived — not committed design)." One-sentence theory: every piece of design knowledge is a "way of looking" (a Lens); there are many possible lenses, not capped; the two recommended for building anything are Realization (abstract idea → running thing) and Reflection (instance → axis → meta-axis → … → grammar). The section self-declares: "This section is THEORY work; productization is the next step."holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-3-how-methodology
§3.1 The atom — a value is just an axismaster-specThe smallest move is classification: a value is an item; an axis is a set of values. The role is RELATIVE to level — "a thing is a VALUE seen from the axis above it; the same thing is an AXIS seen from the values below it." "Value" and "axis" are the same kind of object viewed from one level up or down. This is the only primitive; everything else is this move repeated. Formal grounding cited by the master: type-universe relativity; Tarski's object-language / meta-language stratification.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-3-1-atom
§3.2 Axes — many possible, two recommended; the coupling rulemaster-specAn axis is a particular Lens — one way of looking at the thing being built; there can be MANY axes (viewpoint · concern · time · ownership · scale · certainty · …) and "you are not capped to any number. You pick the lenses that change a decision for this idea and ignore the rest." The two recommended axes: Realization (abstract idea → concrete running thing; example values worldview · vision · strategy · capability · architecture · feature · contract · implementation) and Reflection (instance → axis → meta-axis → method → framework → paradigm → grammar; M0-M6, the recursion uncapped). Other available lenses are added per idea only if they change a decision: viewpoint (user / system / governance), concern (security / cost / accuracy), time (past / now / next / later · v1 / v2 / v3), ownership (human / AI / human-in-loop), scale (fleet / cohort / instance). Coupling of the recommended pair: "as you ascend Reflection to mint a structure (an axis, a schema), the forward Realization journey then consumes that structure instead of re-deriving it. Climb to write a schema once; descend and every instance conforms. The abstraction is carried forward as an input, not copied."holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-3-2-axes
§3.3 Dynamic — nothing is capped; three stop rulesmaster-specThe number of Realization levels and Reflection heights is NOT fixed; it is decided per idea. Dimensions are generated, not listed: each dimension = (interrogative × mechanism) — a finite generator mints an open set on demand. Realization stops by atomicity (downward — stop decomposing at the smallest buildable unit); Reflection stops by reflexivity (upward — stop climbing at the level that describes itself); both stop by the decision test (the practical cap): add a level or a dimension only while it still changes what you build or who reads it. Callout: "Possibility is not obligation. The space is astronomic; you instantiate only the corner a destination needs."holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-3-3-dynamic
§3.4 Worked examples — Realization walk, time axis, nD lenses, 6×7 cross, one-to-many cells, nD projectionmaster-specExample 1 walks one idea down Realization: worldview "operator sovereignty" → vision → strategy → capability → architecture (B1 Intent block inside the CoS layer) → feature (B1 intake: capture → classify → conf-gate → log → route) → contract intent{type, confidence, routed_to, clarify} → implementation (classifier prompt + intake_log.jsonl + emit). Example 2 shows the time axis on the same thing (past: no formal intake · v1: single LLM call, conf-gate 0.6 · v2: eval-tuned 0.75 + golden-set CI · v3: voice + screenshot multi-modal). Example 4 renders the recommended pair as a clean 6×7 cross (Worldview…Implementation × M0 instance…M6 grammar), read left→right as "a concrete thing → the axis it belongs to → the axis-of-axes → the method that produces them → the framework hosting the method → the paradigm → the reflexive grammar." Example 5 states the cross is many-to-many, not 1-to-1 — each cell is a SET of candidates and "a build decision is the act of picking one (or a few) from each relevant cell" — with 5a expanding the M3 method column (e.g. Architecture: C4 model · Zachman framework · 4+1 views · ADR pattern · arc42) and 5b expanding the Architecture row as a fan-out tree across all 7 Reflection levels (M0: 5-layer architecture · 6 surfaces ROUTE/RUN/GATE/EMERGE/AUDIT/TENANT · 10 primitives · 26 EngineEvents · 8 hardstops). Example 6 catalogs six established techniques for showing >2 axes (small multiples/Tufte, parallel coordinates/Inselberg, heatmap, faceted filter/Ranganathan, hierarchical nested headers, 3D cube + slices) with the punchline: "you don't visualize nD directly. You project. Every diagram is a 2D projection of an n-dimensional address space."holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-3-4-examples
§3.5 Decision procedure with Lenses (6 steps + worked decision)master-specA decision is choosing which lens to apply; a good decision is one that changes the path to your destination — "the framework's biggest gift is telling you which decisions you do not have to make yet." The six steps: 1 ANCHOR (name the destination; no destination → no decision) · 2 ON-PATH? (if not in the destination's required sub-graph → DEFER; "Most 'decisions' die here") · 3 LEVEL (find the decision's level/dimension and pick the matching lens — "Decide architecture with the architecture lens, not the implementation lens") · 4 REAL? (do options lead to DIFFERENT downstream sub-graphs? if not, pick either and move on) · 5 WEIGH (reversible → decide fast, revise later; irreversible → apply MULTIPLE lenses; "where the lenses DISAGREE is the actual tradeoff to resolve") · 6 RECORD (node + provenance: destination, lenses used, choice, why). The worked decision "B1 classifier: single LLM call vs fine-tuned model?" resolves to single-call for v1 — reversible-ish (intent{} contract unchanged either way), lenses [cost, time, accuracy, reversibility] agree, with recorded revisit trigger "if eval pass-rate drops below 90%." Net effect per the master: eliminates off-path agonizing (step 2), false decisions (step 4), wrong-level thrash (step 3); adds effort calibrated to reversibility, multi-lens only for the genuinely hard ones, and replayable rationale.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-3-5-decisions-with-lenses
§3.6 The 6 HOW dimensions — operator-facing surfacemaster-specThe 6 dimensions are the operator-facing surface of §3.2's axes — the specific lenses an operator picks when working with Native today; each row reads "what the operator is choosing" and "the values available." Approach: top-down (goal → factors → tasks), bottom-up (data → patterns → conclusion), or by analogy (this looks like prior X). Mindset: optimize (maximize known metric) · explore (surface options) · repair (fix broken state) · audit (verify-only). Structure: tree of sub-tasks (T3 Charter decomposition) · sequential pipeline (T1 multi-action) · parallel scan (T2 iterative) · iteration loop (refinement). Detailing: coarse first pass + sharpen (T0/T1 fast iterations) vs fully detailed from start (T3 charter with locked requirements). Decomposition: 3-factor (forced parsimony) / 7-factor (completeness) / by-stakeholder (comms-heavy) / by-deliverable / by-risk (high-stakes). Cascading: when a sub-task gets stuck, recurse on the sub-task — a sub-T3 spawns a child T3 with its own Lens choices, depth bounded by complexity budget.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-3-6-how-dimensions
§3.7 Lens selection flow → lens manifest in state envelopemaster-specSelection takes operator intent (B1) + task tier (T0-T3) as input. The LENS SELECTION block runs five steps: 1. read Charter scope (§4 primitive) · 2. read past patterns from B5.d · 3. choose lens values (approach · mindset · structure · detailing · decomposition) · 4. estimate complexity → cascade depth · 5. declare lens choice to operator (B3a if explicit; silent if known). Output: "lens manifest emitted into state envelope (§4.3); §4 Native runtime executes." If a sub-task gets stuck the flow recurses lens selection (cascade) with depth--. The master maps this flow onto the §3.5 decision procedure: Anchor → Level → Real? → Weigh → Record.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-3-7-how-selection-flow
§3.8 Cross-section presence — lenses are everywheremaster-specPer the §3.8 table: §2.B B1 (Ask my way) parses the operator's lens preferences from utterance (e.g., "quickly" = coarse-first detailing). §2.B B3a (THINK) brainstorm partnership uses Explore mindset and declares its lens choice before deepening; §2.B B3b (DO) auto-execute uses Optimize mindset by default and switches to Repair when output fails verification. In the §4 Native runtime, the lens manifest in the state envelope binds method routing (§9 D.11), agent scope, and token budget. §4.5 test-before-conclude's verification step is itself a lens choice (audit mindset · by-deliverable decomposition). §6 Distribution/onboarding surfaces + ratifies the operator's first-week lens preferences during Charter setup. §7 Observability carries the Lens accuracy KPI — operator drop rate per lens choice; drift signals on method-route accuracy.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-3-8-how-everywhere
§4.6 HOW Methodology bridge — runtime executor vs §3 doctrinemaster-specMaster §4.6 (cross-reference): "Every Native primitive (§4.1) embeds a HOW choice from §3 HOW Methodology. Charters declare HOW preferences at creation; agents materialize with HOW context inherited; method routing (D.11) implements HOW-tier mapping; authority gate (D.6) defers to HOW's escalation logic. Native runtime is the executor; §3 HOW is the doctrine." The §1 architecture cross-cut table carries the identical contract as its "HOW / method-routing bridge" row, adding that HOW-tier mapping means "different problem classes get different solving strategies, per B3b DO." §2's task-taxonomy callout feeds the same bridge: "Method selection (D.11) chooses different intelligence per tier." D.11 Method Selection itself is a Native platform primitive with its own per-tier method-profile table (T0 speed/fast model · T1 reliability/per-step verify · T2 responsiveness/1-min loop · T3 long-horizon quality/best model + ensemble) — its primary home is master §9 Meta (governance band), so the HOW page cites it rather than restating it.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-6-how-bridge

§3.9 Acceptance criteria (theory v1)master-specForward-looking note verbatim: "this section is THEORY work; productization is the next step. v1 ships the doctrine + the Lens primitive + the recommended axes; v2+ will lock the surface and refine based on dogfood signal." Four criteria: (1) every Native turn carries an explicit lens manifest in state envelope (operator can request "show me your lenses"); (2) lens choices learned from B5.d learned patterns improve future selections (drift signal triggers re-learning); (3) cascade depth bounded by complexity budget (Charter-set); depth-exhaustion escalates to operator (Authority gate); (4) operator can override any lens choice; override learned + applied to future similar tasks.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-3-9-acceptance

DimensionThe question the operator answersExample choices

**Mindset**Optimize · explore · repair · audit?Optimize: maximize known metric · Explore: surface options · Repair: fix broken state · Audit: verify-only
**Structure**Tree of sub-tasks · sequential pipeline · parallel scan · iteration loop?Tree: T3 Charter decomposition · Pipeline: T1 multi-action · Scan: T2 iterative · Loop: refinement

Worked decision — "B1 classifier: single LLM call vs fine-tuned model?" (§3.5 — verbatim)

StepApplied
1 Anchordestination = ship B1 intake (v1)
2 On-path?yes — the classifier is in B1's closure
3 Level / lensL3-component + AI lens — *not* a strategy decision
4 Real?yes — fine-tune pulls in a whole data+training sub-graph; single-call doesn't. Different paths.
5 Weighreversible-ish (the `intent{}` contract is unchanged either way) → lean decide-fast. Lenses: cost (fine-tune expensive), time (single-call ships now), accuracy (eval lens: spike hit 17/20 ≥ threshold). Lenses **agree** → single-call for v1.
6 Recordprovenance: chose single-call; lenses [cost, time, accuracy, reversibility]; revisit if eval pass-rate drops below 90%.

Capturing >2 axes visually (§3.4 Example 6 — verbatim)

TechniqueHow it shows >2 axesSource
Small multiplesa grid of 2D tables, one per value of the 3rd axisTufte
Parallel coordinatesn vertical axes side-by-side; each item is a polyline crossing allInselberg
Heatmap2 axes as grid; 3rd encoded as cell color/intensitystandard
Faceted filter / browsenever show all n; pick values on some axes, see the remaining slice — how nD is actually used in practiceRanganathan
Hierarchical nested headersmulti-level row/column headers stack two axes into one directionstandard table design
3D cube + slicescube for 3 axes; pull 2D slices and lay them out as small multiplesstandard

§7.1 Per-pillar metrics + drift signalsmaster-specEach pillar carries one primary metric and one drift signal: B1 Ask my way — intent parse accuracy (intent_type + sub-intents match operator's correction frequency); correction rate >15% per week → drift. B2 World's best + mine — context relevance (operator drops <10% of retrieved context); drop rate >25% → drift. B3 Think + Do + Present — auto-vs-ask ratio; outcome quality (operator accepts >80% of B3b outputs); accept rate falls >15pp WoW. B4 Talk for me — outbound success rate; audience-calibration accuracy; operator overrides voice >20% of sends. B5 Remember — replay latency (≤30s/day-window v1) + cross-tenant default-deny verified; replay latency >60s OR any cross-tenant leak. §3.8 additionally registers a Lens accuracy KPI in §7: "operator drop rate per lens choice; drift signals on method-route accuracy" (§3's header names §7 as "where Lens accuracy is measured").holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-7-1-metrics
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-3-8-how-everywhere
holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-3-how-methodology
§7.1.A Accuracy KPI — integration with §4.5 test-before-concludemaster-specFour metrics with v1 targets and drift signals: first-time-test pass rate (% of CoS outputs passing the pre-declared §4.5 test on first try) — ≥85% T0, ≥75% T1, ≥65% T2, ≥55% T3 Charter milestones; drift on drop >10pp WoW per task tier. Retry-to-conclude ratio — ≤1.3 T0, ≤2.0 T1, ≤3.0 T2/T3; drift when ratio rises >0.5 WoW. Escalation rate (test-fail-exhausted) — ≤5% across all tiers; >10% any tier → workflow review. Post-conclude operator-correction rate — ≤8% T0/T1, ≤15% T2/T3; rate rises → test criteria need tightening. Callout verbatim: "Accuracy is a per-workflow KPI, not a single number. Each task tier (T0-T3) + each pillar carries its own accuracy band. §4.5 Test-before-conclude is where the test runs; §7.1.A is where the aggregated results live." §4.5's side of the contract: "Accuracy KPI in §7 Observability measures: % of CoS outputs that passed first-time-test vs needed retry." The SoP second-order draft carries this as its outbound Observability contract row, adding "daemon health surfaced only via Daily Pulse health row" and platform-health instrumentation. FQ2 (PROPOSED 2026-05-13, not ratified) holds the forward open questions: KPIs per tier, right test depth per phase, test-before-conclude vs test-by-sample cost tradeoff, accuracy compounding vs degrading over time.holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-7-1-a-accuracy

§7.2 Audit + replay surfaces — the 5-artifact per-turn trailmaster-spec"The audit promise of B5.c made visible. Every Native turn produces 5 artifacts; observability surfaces all 5 to operator on demand": 1. INTENT RECORD (B1 — parsed intent + sub-intents + schema preview) · 2. CONTEXT MANIFEST (B2 — ranked artifact list used; what was dropped) · 3. DECISION PROVENANCE (B3 — phases + factors + authority verdict + output) · 4. OUTBOUND LOG (B4, if comms triggered — audience + channel + draft + sent + replies) · 5. SUBSTRATE WRITE (B5 — active-session ref + learned-pattern updates). Operator commands: "replay last Paisa turn" → all 5 artifacts shown chronologically; "show me what changed in B5.d this week" → diff of learned patterns. The SoR second-order draft mirrors this as a stored-data row ("Per-turn 5-artifact audit trail — every Native turn produces 5 stored artifacts") and as its outbound Observability contract row: all 5 per-turn artifacts replayed on demand, replay ≤30s for a single-day window, learned-pattern diffs, B5 drift signals (replay latency >60s OR any cross-tenant leak).holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-7-2-audit

§7.3 Operator satisfaction signalsmaster-specFour signals, verbatim from master: time-to-decision (operator decides on CoS output in <30s — CX bar gate); refusal-to-execute rate (operator says "don't do this, ask me first"); autonomy-dial direction (does operator dial autonomy up or down per domain over time?); surface-switching frequency (operator should NOT switch surfaces mid-task per CX bar).holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-7-3-satisfaction
§7.4 Acceptance criteria (Observability v1) + the HS-4 dependencymaster-specThree criteria: (1) 5-artifact audit trail emitted per turn — HS-4 fires on any missing channel; (2) operator can request replay for any past turn within ≤30s for a single-day window; (3) drift signals tracked weekly; breach surfaces to founder via Daily Pulse. HS-4 canon (primary home: platform-runtime hardstops; cited here because §7.4 depends on it): fires when the DecisionProvenance JSONL audit log is unwritable across all 3 channels — primary fsync'd append (ADR-013), stderr beacon, /tmp dual fallback; fail-mode "block all governance hooks; stderr beacon"; downstream, "audit-trail integrity preserved by blocking, not by writing without trace"; STRIDE class Repudiation / audit-trail loss. Master carries the MECHANISM-PENDING hedge (added 2026-05-13 per §10.10 row 1): HS-4..HS-8 "are documented as canonical hardstops... but the impl-vs-docs audit confirmed live detectors/executors do NOT yet exist in the shipped Native runtime — those rows are doctrine, mechanism pending."holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-7-4-acceptance

AUDIT surface — canon persistence + replay subsystemnative-canonCanon: "Every state transition across every surface emits exactly one typed EngineEvent + (for consequential decisions) one DecisionProvenance row. AUDIT is the persistence + replay surface for those rows." AUDIT is not directly invoked — ROUTE/RUN/GATE/EMERGE/TENANT call UserKit.appendDecisionProvenance(dp) (§3.1) or emit EngineEvent rows AUDIT persists. Read-side surfaces: DecisionProvenance JSONL (append-only telemetry sink §5.6); EngineEvent JSONL per Tenant at Tenant.audit_log_path (§2.8); replay surface §6.1 — every consequential decision rebuildable from agent_identity + policy_id + policy_version + data_refs + outcome. Eight invariants AUDIT-I1..I8: ≥1 EngineEvent per transition; exactly one DecisionProvenance per consequential decision (I-7); ts_ms monotonic per process; reason field sanitized (no colons, no newlines) for JSONL parseability; durability via fsync per append + 3-channel fallback + stderr beacon last-resort; HS-4 fail-CLOSED ("if AUDIT cannot write, governance halts"); policy_id + policy_version non-empty on every row (F-8 + I-9); agent_identity chain-shaped per ADR-015. AUDIT persists all 26 EngineEvent types of §3.2 (full catalog enumerated in the part-file; one canon part-file per event exists under sutra/os/native/events/). Stated canon gaps: cross-process replay deferred per §8 OS-3; no audit_log_write_failed event type in §3.2 (HS-4 uses stderr beacon, not an EngineEvent).sutra/os/native/surfaces/audit.md
sutra/os/native/primitives/engine-event.md
sutra/os/native/primitives/decision-provenance.md
sutra/os/native/events/ (26 part-files, one per EngineEvent type)
ADR-013 — telemetry sink: fsync'd JSONL appendadr-decisionDecision: "Native engine MUST persist EngineEvent and DecisionProvenance as fsync'd JSONL append-only files; H-Sutra event bus MUST follow the same shape." Mechanics: one JSONL line per event/decision; UTF-8; no newline-in-field (sanitized at emit); fsync per append — never lose a row on crash; stderr beacon fallback when primary path is unwritable; dual fallback to /tmp (HS-4 chain); the pattern detector (ADR-010) reads the log directly — no in-memory cache, restart-safe. Alternatives rejected: in-memory ring buffer (fails restart-survival — codex P1.2 forced rejection) and SQLite/DB (adds dependency + schema migrations). Consequences: crash-safe; zero new runtime dependency; multi-process safe (POSIX append atomicity for short lines); disk I/O cost per event accepted at current rate; no indexed query — replay is sequential scan, aggregation is downstream tooling; OTel production transport (OS-10) is the next-tier deferral, JSONL stays primary.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-013-telemetry-sink-fsync-jsonl.md
North Star metric — Operator-Hours-Saved per week (OHS/wk)native-canonCanon definition: "Hours per week the operator NO LONGER thinks about, because Native handled them — audit-derived from auto-run lifecycle phases + operator weekly confirmation." Why this metric: captures unit-of-value (time); measurable; leading indicator of retention; aligns with JTBD outcomes #1+#2 (minimize time + variance); works at any scale. Two-channel measurement per NATIVE-ENGINE §11.2: (a) audit-derived — auto-run lifecycle Executions × completion-time × non-trivial gate; (b) operator self-report — weekly survey. Cadence: weekly aggregation; per-operator + per-tenant roll-up. Leading inputs: workflows successfully auto-run after operationalization (count/wk), pattern proposals approved (precision ≥75%), cross-company decisions replayable from audit log ≥99% (DecisionProvenance log completeness 100% of consequential decisions), operator weekly active sessions trending up over 12 weeks, time-to-first-Execution ≤30 min (I-11). Targets table compiled below; 5-year targets marked "illustrative for direction; founder reviews + tunes at quarterly OKR cycle."sutra/os/native/metrics/north-star-ohs-per-week.md

Coverage engine — shipped five-layer process-coverage system (production dossier)shipped-productionProduction ships a complete, live process-coverage system in five layers: (1) spec — COVERAGE-ENGINE.md with a depth-indexed expected checklist (D1=4, D2=7, D3=14, D4=19, D5=24 method IDs), the coverage-log.jsonl row schema {task, depth, method, ts, evidence, skipped_reason, override}, the evidence-specificity rule ("if your evidence could describe any task, it's not evidence"), and a SOFT-to-HARD ladder; (2) machine-readable contract — os/method-registry.jsonl encoding all 26 methods with id/category/min_depth/required_at[depths]/phase/conditional; (3) writers — log-coverage.sh HARD-rejects any runtime skip (5th arg → exit 2, per D31 "clients execute, never skip") and auto-coverage.sh fires on PostToolUse(Edit|Write|Bash), declaratively mapping paths/commands to methods with per-turn dedupe (the dominant production writer, not LLM discipline); (4) gate + report — coverage-gate.sh at Stop computes expected = enabled_methods ∩ required_at[depth] and prints the missing list SOFT (always exit 0); coverage-report.sh emits OK/SKIP/MISS/COND with status bands (EXCELLENT≥90 / GOOD≥70 / CONCERN≥50 / CRITICAL<50), bonus methods never inflating the score; (5) aggregation — observability-synthesis.sh (3h launchd) derives coverage-fires-per-commit, RED when commits>0 but fires=0. Redo recipe for Native: port the method registry verbatim as a Native SoR table; reimplement the writer as EngineEvent emission in the daemon (declarative path-glob/command-substring → method data, daemon-owned dedupe); reimplement gate + report as queries over events; keep SOFT-first with codified HARD triggers (per-method fire rate >85% stable 90 days → individually HARD-gate; <60% session coverage → block checkpoint); "surface coverage-per-commit as the §7 Observability process-adherence KPI." Dossier confidence: high; live log verified (43 rows, latest 2026-06-11).holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#engine/coverage
Coverage enablement authority — D31 (production dossier)shipped-productionThe D31 dossier mechanizes "only Sutra flips switches": a per-client enabled_methods block in os/SUTRA-CONFIG.md is marked "Sutra-managed, do not edit manually," defaults false for every new method (clients with no block = all methods disabled), with the live asawa instance enabling all 26 registry methods. log-coverage.sh had its runtime skip path REMOVED 2026-04-20 — passing a 5th (skip_reason) argument exits 2 with stderr instructing the client to either fire the method or file feedback at os/feedback-to-sutra/; "clients literally cannot skip at runtime." coverage-gate.sh runs at Stop computing enabled_methods ∩ required_at_depth — SOFT Phase 1, with HARD Phase 2 (Stop-event block + COVERAGE_OVERRIDE=1 audited escape) and Phase 3 telemetry-driven review specified. Redo recipe: per-tenant capability switches (default false) in platform-owned tenant config; an execution logger with no runtime-skip parameter (port the reject-skip block verbatim — "it is the heart of the direction"); do NOT port PROTO-018 (retired) — redesign flip distribution on Native's plugin-version push; keep the three-phase SOFT→HARD→telemetry hardening schedule. Gap vs Native: only a one-line clause in section-10.5; the mechanism is unspecified in the runtime and section-6.7; maps to requirement N36.holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#dir/D31
Fleet consumption dashboard — fleet.sh (production dossier)shipped-productionProduction ships holding/departments/analytics/fleet.sh (178 lines) — "the 'who is running the plugin, right now' dashboard." Data source: ~/.sutra/sutra-data-cache/ (local clone of private repo sankalpasawa/sutra-data), populated by client /sutra-push events from telemetry-opt-in installs, each contributing clients/<install_id>/manifest.json; rows parse to {install_id, github_login, git_user_name, os_pretty, arch, tz, project_name, push_count, sutra_version, last_seen, age_days, has_identity}. Three modes: all installs (default), --fresh (last_seen ≤7 days = active), --id <prefix> single-install detail; output is 5 aggregates (total installs, identified count/%, unique github logins, unique humans, total pushes) plus a per-install table, with a printed privacy caveat — outbound telemetry DISABLED by default since plugin v2.0.0 per PRIVACY.md. Speed charter — latency profiling + governance-overhead budget (production dossier)shipped-productionProduction ships a three-part solution: (1) SPEED.md charter (ACTIVE) with the Iron Rule "No Speed improvement ships without a cited time-sink baseline from this charter's data. Guess-driven cuts are blocked," 8 KPI formulas with Warn/Breach thresholds (governance overhead % = sum of routing+depth+hook+boot phases / task wall time, target <15%, breach ≥30% auto-becomes the #1 cut), a 6-wave gated roadmap (taxonomy → instrument → collect 2 weeks zero-change → RCA → founder-approved proposals → ship+measure → propagate), an anti-pattern table (cache-by-feel, single-run optimization, killing governance for speed all banned), and a satisfied-closure clause; (2) SPEED-phase-taxonomy.md with the full phase tree, per-phase start/end boundary-event table, governance-overhead summation formula, 8 collector invariants (I1: sum(phase_ms) within ±2% of wall), and the canonical LATENCY-LOG.jsonl row schema; (3) latency-collector.sh — a Stop-hook collector appending one row per task (wall_ms, tool counts, full token usage incl. cache_read/cache_creation, partial flag). Operational state per the dossier: DORMANT — the holding copy is absent, the plugin copy is unregistered in hooks.json, and LATENCY-LOG.jsonl is frozen at 10 rows (all 2026-04-20); waves W2+ never completed. Redo recipe: port the taxonomy, formulas, invariants, row schema, Iron Rule, and anti-pattern table verbatim ("the measured-universe IP... implementation-independent"); redesign the collector to emit latency rows from Native's own engine events into the ADR-013 fsync-JSONL telemetry sink "so measurement cannot silently decouple from execution"; wire D21's speed_weight into the depth/estimation flow as a real field. Gap vs Native: only scattered point latency targets exist (B5 replay ≤30s, pulse gen ≤60s, CX-bar ≤2min) plus D.11 per-tier targets and the D21 doctrine row; HLD/07 Non-Functional fill remains a TODO (T-G8-08 — "perf · security · observability · accuracy · reliability · cost · resilience").holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/SHIPPED-SOLUTIONS.md#charter/speed

Accuracy KPI (§7.1.A — verbatim)

MetricDefinitionTarget v1Drift signal
First-time-test pass rate% of CoS outputs that pass the pre-declared §4.5 test on first try (no retry needed)≥85% T0, ≥75% T1, ≥65% T2, ≥55% T3 Charter milestonesDrop >10pp WoW per task tier
Retry-to-conclude ratioAvg retries per concluded task (test-fail → B3 retry loop iterations)≤1.3 T0, ≤2.0 T1, ≤3.0 T2/T3Ratio rises >0.5 WoW
Escalation rate (test-fail-exhausted)% of tasks where retry budget exhausted → operator escalation≤5% across all tiers>10% any tier → workflow review
Post-conclude operator-correction rate% of CoS-concluded outputs operator subsequently corrects (test missed the failure)≤8% T0/T1, ≤15% T2/T3Rate rises → test criteria need tightening

engine/coverage gap (dossier): Native names the Coverage Engine only in the §11.3 engines file listing — missing all five shipped layers and any process-adherence metric in §7 Observability (production's coverage-per-commit RED/GREEN signal); 'Native's per-turn hooks enforce marker presence but measure nothing about session-level process completion.'
cap/100 gap (dossier): the fleet-level aggregate consumption view (identity/version/recency/push-count aggregates + active-fleet filter) is unspecified in Native; only partner-fleet observability appears, and only as a backlog criterion.
charter/speed gap (dossier): Native carries only scattered point latency targets (B5 replay ≤30s, pulse gen ≤60s, CX-bar ≤2min) — missing per-task phase-resolution profiling, the where-time-goes RCA framework, the <15% governance-overhead budget with computable formula, and the wave-gated no-building-before-data discipline; production pipeline itself is DORMANT (collector absent/unregistered, LATENCY-LOG frozen at 10 rows). HLD/07 Non-Functional fill remains TODO — T-G8-08, which FQ2 Accuracy KPIs partially blocks (chains to T-G4-04..06).
dir/D31 gap (dossier): per-client switches defaulting false, Sutra-only flips, removed client skip path, coverage-gate SOFT→HARD — unspecified in the Native runtime and section-6.7; maps to requirement N36.
AUDIT canon gaps (surfaces/audit.md): cross-process replay deferred per §8 OS-3 (within-process only in v1); no audit_log_write_failed event type in §3.2 — future ADR may codify an audit_failure event if cross-process replay lands.
HS-4 canon gaps (hardstops/HS-4-audit-unwritable.md): exact identity of 'the 3 channels' beyond primary/stderr/tmp NOT further specified; whether 'all governance hooks' blocking is session-wide or process-wide NOT specified; specific restore procedures and partial-recovery semantics NOT specified — all marked 'runtime implementation choice; future ADR may codify.'
ADR-013 deferral: no indexed query — replay is sequential scan, aggregation is downstream tooling; OTel production transport (OS-10) is the next-tier deferral.
FQ2 (§C.6) PROPOSED 2026-05-13, not ratified: accuracy KPIs per task tier, right test depth per phase, test-before-conclude vs test-by-sample cost tradeoff, accuracy compounding vs degrading — observability home named in its touch column (§7 + HS-1/HS-4).

Cell tag semantics — PRIMARY / SUPPORTING / emptysecond-order-draftThe legend fixes three cell states: PRIMARY — "the pillar's home block(s)"; SUPPORTING — "the pillar traverses or depends on this block"; empty — "no stated involvement in the doc". B1 traversal — one pillar as one vertical slice through the horizontal blockssecond-order-draft"One pillar = one vertical slice through the horizontal blocks." The wired B1 traversal: intent can arrive from External World "via Slack / email / web (N07)", crossing the Authority + Tenancy gate at the boundary edge; the UI receives the "freeform utterance" and the Host echoes "parsed intent + schema preview" back to the UI; intent parse flows Host→Orchestration as "model work on Host (schema pre-declared before the LLM call)"; Orchestration hands "typed intent + sub-intents" / "task = process" to System of Process; SoP "writes intake artifacts to B5.a (active session)" and "reads B5 for shorthand interpretation" from System of Record. A dashed gray container groups Orchestration + System of Process as the DAEMON, and "both the Daemon container and the Host carry their own runs-on edge to Compute". B1's PRIMARY blocks draw strong blue (UI labeled "B1 interaction strip", Orchestration labeled "translate · find the process"); External World and Compute draw dashed as neighbor and substrate, with the Compute strip noting "no B1-specific compute fact stated in the doc".holding/website/native/_matrix.html (traversal SVG + svgnote, lines 135-221)

§1.0 locked first-layer schema — the matrix's column source and edge vocabularymaster-spec§1.0 System architecture is the runtime at a glance: "The operator asks through the UI; the request runs on the Host (Claude CLI — agent runtime + model in one); inside the always-on Daemon, Orchestration translates the request and finds the relevant process to run; the System of Process runs it (this is the runtime), gathering context from the System of Record + the External World and writing output back to the System of Record. Authority + Tenancy gates every access; Host + daemon run on the Compute (host machine). Second-level detail (context-build · verify · connectors) lives inside System of Process — see §4." The matrix's crumb restates this first layer ("Human → UI → Host → Daemon{Orchestration → System of Process} → SoR / External World") and its foot fixes "columns in pipeline order per sec.1.0". The matrix's B2/B3/B4 PRIMARY-cell statements lean on §1.0 placements: "Context-build lives inside SoP (§1.0)", "Connectors live inside SoP (§1.0)", and the "SoP↔External World arrow carries 'context + effects'".holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-1-0-runtime (lines 819-865)
holding/website/native/_matrix.html (crumb line 50; foot line 268; cells lines 85, 101, 109)
§4.1 Step primitive — the doc's only LLM-call site (cross-referenced by matrix gap #2)master-spec§4.1 defines Step as the "Atomic unit of a Workflow — pre-declared expected output + LLM call OR deterministic action + post-call check (per P2 pre/post-LLM-validation). Step is where work happens", doc-homed at primitives/step.md with ADR-012 PNC typed parser. The matrix's own gap quotes this definition: "Sec.4.1 puts LLM calls inside Workflow Steps (Step = 'LLM call OR deterministic action... where work happens' — SoP runtime)... yet B1's parse contract ('pre-declared output schema shown before LLM call') sits in Orchestration's translate step". The System of Process second-order draft states the same tension: "The Step primitive (sec.4.1) is the doc's only LLM-call site, which implies System of Process is the Host caller; an explicit only-SoP-calls-Host rule is not yet stated anywhere in the doc."holding/website/native/master/index.html#section-4-1-primitives (Step row)
holding/website/native/_matrix.html (gap bullet, line 261)
holding/website/native/master/index.html#so-system-of-process (line 1758 Host contract row; line 1782 open question)
ADR-005 host-LLM host selection — the per-workflow host decision named by matrix gap #2adr-decisionADR-005 (Accepted 2026-05-07) decides: "Native engine MUST require WorkflowStep.host ∈ {claude, codex} whenever action='invoke_host_llm'." host='claude' invokes claude --bare — recursion-safe, default for steps that must execute in the founder's session via the effector split (ADR-004); host='codex' invokes codex exec — read-only sandbox, default for analysis, review, planning steps that produce JSON/text output without state change. Per-step timeout_ms flows into host activity args; host-class default is 60s for claude --bare, ≥300s for codex. The tradeoff is locked to these two hosts in v1.x; the LLMSubstrate adapter for OpenAI/Gemini swap is deferred to the v1.x backlog. The matrix's gap #2 names this ADR — "ADR-005 selects the host-LLM per workflow" — when asking "which block issues B1's intent-parse LLM call?"sutra/os/decisions/ADR-005-host-llm-host-selection.md#decision

PRIMARY cell statements (verbatim)

CellStatement
B1 × UIFreeform utterance enters here; parsed intent + schema preview echo back on the same surface within one turn (B1 interaction strip).
B1 × OrchestrationTranslates the freeform request into typed intent + sub-intents and finds the relevant process — the intent_type handoff.
B2 × System of ProcessContext-build lives inside SoP (§1.0): gathers + ranks the three concentric sources (my own / world's best / peer Sutras v2+) within declared budget.
B3 × System of ProcessTHE runtime for the action axis: THINK / DO / PRESENT end-to-end, self-checks before presenting (N03), idea-to-standing-pattern.
B4 × External WorldMessages land with the operator's people + the world via Slack / email / web; replies re-enter via B3; peer-Native A2A v2+ (v1 log-only stub).
B5 × System of RecordB5 IS the system-of-record: every pillar B1–B4 writes here; DecisionProvenance, ≤30s replay, multi-task state, learned patterns (B5.a–B5.d).
B5 × Authority + TenancyGovernance axis: whose data lives where + who reads across tenant boundaries; cross-tenant queries default-deny; N06 "my data stays mine".

Legend (verbatim)

TagMeaning
PRIMARYthe pillar's home block(s)
SUPPORTINGthe pillar traverses or depends on this block
emptyno stated involvement in the doc
All 5 pillars' Ring 1 Surface rows (Problem space / Need-outcome / Solution direction) are 'Awaiting founder fill' (T-G1-02..T-G1-16) — could founder voice shift any PRIMARY assignment once filled?
Sec.4.1 puts LLM calls inside Workflow Steps (Step = 'LLM call OR deterministic action... where work happens' — SoP runtime) and ADR-005 selects the host-LLM per workflow, yet B1's parse contract ('pre-declared output schema shown before LLM call') sits in Orchestration's translate step — which block issues B1's intent-parse LLM call?
Section 1.0 gives Compute no pillar-differentiated role — does B5 'keep it mine' imply local-compute data residency on the host machine, or is Compute a uniform substrate for all pillars?
B3b DO external effects: the section-1-0-runtime diagram labels the SoP-to-External-World edge 'context + effects', but B3's Connectedness routes external touches only via B4 — do non-comms tool effects bypass B4 or traverse it?
Inbound B4 responses 're-enter via B3 (next action)' — does re-entry traverse Orchestration as a fresh translate, or go straight into the running System of Process? Doc is silent.
B5 says 'operator can write standing prefs directly' — the write path (UI direct to System of Record vs routed through System of Process) is unspecified in the cited ranges.
